symmetric nondegenerate bilinear form

B|_{\mathfrak{h}}
on
\mathfrak{h}
, so that
H
is a
reductive subgroup of
G
.

We still assume
\mathfrak{h}
to be
\theta
-stable. Let
Z_{G}\left(H\right)\subset G
be the centralizer of
H
, and
let
N_{G}\left(H\right)\subset G
be its normalizer. Then
Z_{G}\left(H\right)
is included in
H
, it is just the
center
Z\left(H\right)
of
H
. As in
[
Kn86
, p. 131]
, the analytic Weyl group

W\left(H:G\right)
is defined
as the quotient

(3.52)

W\left(H:G\right)=N_{G}\left(H\right)/Z_{G}\left(H\right).

Put

(3.53)

\displaystyle Z_{K}\left(H\right)=Z_{G}\left(H\right)\cap K,

\displaystyle N_{K}\left(H\right)=N_{G}\left(H\right)\cap K.

Then
N_{K}\left(H\right)/Z_{K}\left(H\right)
embeds in

W\left(H:G\right)
. By
[
Kn86
, p. 131]
, this embedding is an
isomorphism, i.e.,

(3.54)

W\left(H:G\right)=N_{K}\left(H\right)/Z_{K}\left(H\right).

By

[
Kn86
, eq. (5.6)]
,
W\left(H:G\right)\subset W\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
.

By
[
Kn86
, p. 130]
, an element
\gamma\in G
is said to be regular if

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
is a Cartan subalgebra. If
H
is the corresponding Cartan subgroup, then

\gamma\in H
. By
[
Kn86
, Theorem
5.22]
, the set

G^{\mathrm{reg}}\subset G
of regular
elements is open and
conjugation invariant. More precisely, if
H_{1},\ldots,H_{\ell}

denotes
the finite family of nonconjugate Cartan subgroups, by
[
Kn86
, Theorem
5.22]
,
G^{\mathrm{reg}}
splits as the disjoint union of open sets

(3.55)

G^{\mathrm{reg}}=\cup_{i=1}^{\ell}G^{\mathrm{reg}}_{H_{i}},

where
G^{\mathrm{reg}}_{H_{i}}
denote the open set of elements of

G^{\mathrm{reg}}
that are conjugate to an element of
H_{i}
.

If
\gamma\in H
,
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
acts on
\mathfrak{g}
and fixes
\mathfrak{h}
. Since
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
preserves
B
, it also acts on

\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}
, so that
1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
acts on

\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}
. Then
\gamma
is regular if and only this
endomorphism is invertible, i.e.,

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\neq 0
.

3.8.
Cartan subgroups and semisimple elements

The following result is established in
[
Va77
, Part I, Section 2.3,
Theorem 4]
.

Proposition 3.16
.

A group element
\gamma\in G
is semisimple if and only if it
lies in a Cartan subgroup.

Let us give a direct proof of part of our proposition. Le
\mathfrak{h}
be a

\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra, and let
H
be the corresponding
Cartan subgroup. If
\gamma\in H
, then
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. Moreover,
\gamma
can be written uniquely
in the form

(3.56)

\displaystyle\gamma=e^{a}k^{-1},

\displaystyle a\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}},\qquad k\in H\cap K.

Since
a\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}},k\in H
, then

\mathrm{Ad}\left(k\right)a=a
, which guarantees that
\gamma
is semisimple in

G
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be a Cartan subalgebra, and let

H\subset G
be the associated Cartan subgroup. If
\gamma\in H
, then
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, so that
\mathfrak{h}
is a Cartan subalgebra
of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. In particular
G
and

Z^{0}\left(\gamma\right)
have the same complex rank.

Proposition 3.17
.

Any
\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
is also a Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
.

Proof.

As we saw in Subsection
2.2
,

Z^{0}\left(\gamma\right)
is a connected reductive group and

\theta
induces on
Z^{0}\left(\gamma\right)
a corresponding
Cartan involution. Since
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
is commutative and

\theta
-stable, and since its action on
\mathfrak{g}
preserves

B
, it acts on
\mathfrak{g}
by semisimple
endomorphisms of
\mathfrak{g}
. Since
G
and

Z^{0}\left(\gamma\right)
have the same complex rank,
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
is a Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

3.9.
Root systems and their characters

Let
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be a
\theta
-stable Cartan
subalgebra. We use the notation of the previous subsections.

Take
\gamma\in H
. As we saw after Proposition

3.16
, if
\gamma\in H
, we can write
\gamma
uniquely in
the form

(3.57)

\displaystyle\gamma=e^{a}k^{-1},

\displaystyle a\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}},\qquad k\in H\cap K,

so that

(3.58)

\mathrm{Ad}\left(k\right)a=a.

Let
R\left(\gamma\right),R\left(a\right)
be the root systems
associated with
\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right),\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right)\right)
. We will denote with extra subscripts the
corresponding real, imaginary, and complex roots.

Theorem 3.18
.

If
\gamma\in H
, for any
\alpha\in R
,
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)

preserves the
1
-dimensional complex line
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
. For every
\alpha\in R
, there is a character

\xi_{\alpha}:H\to\mathbf{C}^{*}
such that
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)

acts on
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
by multiplication by

\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)
. If
\alpha\in R
,

(3.59)

\xi_{\alpha}\xi_{-\alpha}=1.

If
\alpha\in R
, if
f\in\mathfrak{h},k\in H\cap K
, then

(3.60)

\displaystyle\xi_{\alpha}\left(e^{f}\right)=e^{\left\langle\alpha,f\right\rangle},

\displaystyle\left|\xi_{\alpha}\left(k\right)\right|=1.

In particular, if
\gamma\in H
is taken as in (
3.57
), then

(3.61)

\displaystyle\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=e^{\left\langle\alpha,a\right\rangle}\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\xi_{-\theta\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=\overline{\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)}.

If
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}
, then

\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\in\mathbf{R}^{*}
, if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}
, then
\left|\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right|=1
. If

\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}
, the restriction of
\xi_{\alpha}
to

H\cap K
takes its values in
\left\{-1,+1\right\}
.

Also

(3.62)

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}=\prod_{\alpha\in R}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right),

and
\gamma
is regular if and only if for any
\alpha\in R
,

\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\neq 1
.

The following identities hold:

\displaystyle R\left(\gamma\right)=\left\{\alpha\in R,\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=1\right\},\qquad R\left(a\right)=\left\{\alpha\in R,\left\langle\alpha,a\right\rangle=0\right\},

(3.63)

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{re}}\left(\gamma\right)=R\left(\gamma\right)\cap R^{\mathrm{re}},\,\,R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(\gamma\right)=R\left(\gamma\right)\cap R^{\mathrm{im}},\,\,R^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\gamma\right)=R\left(\gamma\right)\cap R^{\mathrm{c}},

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{re}}\left(a\right)=R\left(a\right)\cap R^{\mathrm{re}},\,\,R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(a\right)=R\left(a\right)\cap R^{\mathrm{im}},\,\,R^{\mathrm{c}}\left(a\right)=R\left(a\right)\cap R^{\mathrm{c}}.

Also
R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)=R\left(\gamma\right)\cap R_{+},R_{+}\left(a\right)=R\left(a\right)\cap R_{+}
are positive root
systems
for
\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right),\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right)\right)
.

Proof.

If
\gamma\in H
, then
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)

fixes
\mathfrak{h}
, and so if
h\in\mathfrak{h}
, we have the
commutation relation in
\mathrm{End}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,

(3.64)

\left[\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right),\mathrm{ad}\left(h\right)\right]=0.

By (
3.7
),
(
3.64
), we deduce that for any
\alpha\in R
,

\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
preserves
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
. Since

\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
is a complex line,
H
acts on
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
via a character
\xi_{\alpha}
.

Since
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
preserves
B
, if
f,f^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}
, we get

(3.65)

B\left(\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)f,f^{\prime}\right)=B\left(f,\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)^{-1}f^{\prime}\right).

Take
\alpha\in R
. By (
3.65
), if
f\in\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},f^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}_{-\alpha}
, then

(3.66)

\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)B\left(f,f^{\prime}\right)=\xi_{-\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)B\left(f,f^{\prime}\right).

As we saw in Subsection
3.3
, if
\alpha\in R
, the
pairing between
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{-\alpha}
via

B
is nondegenerate. By (
3.66
), we get (
3.59
).

The first equation
in (
3.60
) is trivial. Since
\xi_{\alpha}
restricts to a character
of the compact group
H\cap K
, we get the second equation in
(
3.60
). The first equation in (
3.61
) follows from the
previous considerations. Since

\theta\left(\gamma\right)=e^{-a}k^{-1}
, and since
\theta
maps

\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
into
\mathfrak{g}_{\theta\alpha}
, we obtain the second
equation in (
3.61
). From this second equation, we deduce
that if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}
, then

\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)
is real, and if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}
, then
\left|\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right|=1
. If
\gamma\in H\cap K,\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}
, we know that
\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\in\mathbf{R}^{*},\left|\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right|=1
, so
that
\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=\pm 1
.

Equations (
3.62
), (
3.18
) are trivial. By
(
3.62
),
\gamma
is regular if and only if for
\alpha\in R
,
\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\neq 1
.

Now we proceed as in

[
BL99
, Theorem 1.38]
. If
\kappa\subset\mathfrak{h}
is a positive Weyl chamber for
\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
, the forms in
R
do not vanish on
\kappa
, so
that
\kappa
is included in a
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
Weyl
chamber. It follows that
R\left(\gamma\right)\cap R_{+}
is a
positive root system on
\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
. The same argument is valid for

R\left(a\right)
.
The proof of our
theorem is completed.
∎

3.10.
Real roots, imaginary roots, and semisimple elements

We still take
\gamma
as in Subsection
3.9
.
When taking the intersection of
\mathfrak{i},\mathfrak{r},\mathfrak{c}
with

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right),\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right),\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)
, this will be indicated with a parenthesis
containing the corresponding argument. The intersection with

\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right),\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right),\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(k\right)
will be
denoted with an extra
\perp
. These vector spaces also have a
\mathfrak{p}
and a

\mathfrak{k}
component.

By construction,

(3.67)

R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(\gamma\right)=R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right).

As in (
3.25
), (
3.26
), we get

(3.68)

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(\gamma\right)=R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(\gamma\right),

\displaystyle R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)=R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(k\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(k\right).

To make the notation simpler, in (
3.68
), we did not use
instead the notation
\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right),\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right),\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right),\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)
.

Proposition 3.19
.

The following identities holds:

(3.69)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{i}\left(k\right),\qquad\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}\left(k\right)\subset\mathfrak{z}_{a}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right).

Also

(3.70)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}\left(k\right)_{\mathbf{C}}=\oplus_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}\left(k\right)_{\mathbf{C}}=\oplus_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right)_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\bigoplus\oplus_{\alpha\in R\left(a\right)}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},

(3.71)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)\subset\mathfrak{r}\oplus\mathfrak{c},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)_{\mathbf{C}}=\oplus_{\alpha\in R\setminus R\left(a\right)}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}.

If
\gamma
is regular, then

(3.72)

\mathfrak{i}\left(k\right)=0.

Proof.

By the first identity in (
3.21
), since
a\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, we get the first identity in (
3.69
).
Combining the third identity in (
2.20
) with this
first identity, we
get the second identity in (
3.69
). The third
identity in (
3.69
) is a consequence of the first two.
By (
3.7
), we get (
3.70
) and the first and the
third equations in
(
3.19
), the second equation being a consequence of
the first equation in (
3.69
). Also

\gamma
is regular if and only if
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{h}
. Since
\mathfrak{h}\cap\mathfrak{i}=0
, by the second identity in (
3.69
), we get (
3.72
).
The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

3.11.
Cartan subalgebras and differential operators

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a
\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra. There is a natural
projection
\mathfrak{g}^{*}\to\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

By (
3.5
), there is a well-defined projection
\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{h}
. To the splitting (
3.5
) corresponds the
dual splitting

(3.73)

\mathfrak{g}^{*}=\mathfrak{h}^{*}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{*\perp}.

The projections
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)\to S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}\right),S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\to S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)
associated with (
3.5
), (
3.73
)
are just the restriction

r
of
polynomials on
\mathfrak{g}
to
\mathfrak{h}
, or of
polynomials on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
to
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
.

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
acts as an algebra of derivations on

S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)
.

Definition 3.20
.

Let

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)\subset S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)

be the algebra of invariant elements in
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)
, i.e., the algebra of the elements of

S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)
on which the derivations
associated with
\mathfrak{g}
vanish. Let

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
be the
algebra of
W\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
-invariant elements in
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
.

Recall that

(3.74)

S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\right)=S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}\right)_{\mathbf{C}}.

In particular
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\right)
is equipped
with a natural conjugation.

Proposition 3.21
.

The algebra
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\right)
is preserved under complex conjugation. There is a real
algebra

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*},\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)\subset S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}\right)
such that

(3.75)

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)=I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*},\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)_{\mathbf{C}}.

The map
r:S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)\to S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}\right)
induces the canonical isomorphism

(3.76)

r:I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*},\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right).

Proof.

By Proposition
3.1
,
W\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
is preserved by conjugation. Therefore

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\right)
is
preserved by conjugation, which gives (
3.75
). From the obvious isomorphism

(3.77)

r:I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\right)\to I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}\right),

we get (
3.76
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

What we did for
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
can also be done for
\mathfrak{g}
.
The same argument as in (
3.76
) leads to the identification

(3.78)

r:I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right).

As we saw in Subsection
3.1
,
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
can be identified with the algebra

D^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
of real differential operators on

\mathfrak{g}
with constant coefficients, so
that
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
is identified with the algebra

D_{I}^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
of real
differential operators with constant coefficients on
\mathfrak{g}

which commute with the above
\mathfrak{g}
-derivations.
Similarly
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
can be
identified with the algebra

D^{\cdot}_{I}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
of real differential
operators on
\mathfrak{h}
with constant coefficients that are
W\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
-invariant.

Let
R_{+}\subset R
be a positive root system as in Subsection

3.5
. Recall that the associated polynomial
\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}}\in S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
was defined in
(
3.41
).
If
A\in I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)=D_{I}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
, if

f\in I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)
, then
Af\in I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)
, so that
r\left(Af\right)\in I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*},\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)
. Also

r\left(A\right)\in I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)=D_{I}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
. By

[
HC57a
, Lemmas 6 and 8]
, if
f\in I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right)
,

(3.79)

r\left(Af\right)=\frac{1}{\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}}}r\left(A\right)\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}}rf.

Let
C^{\infty,\mathfrak{g}}\left(\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{R}\right)
be the vector space of
smooth real functions on
\mathfrak{g}
that vanish under the above

\mathfrak{g}
-derivations. Then
(
3.79
) extends to
f\in C^{\infty,\mathfrak{g}}\left(\mathfrak{g},\mathbf{R}\right)
.

4.
Root systems and the function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}

The purpose of this Section is to give a drastically simplified
version of the function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
introduced in
Definition
2.6
. This will be done by expressing this
function in terms of a positive root system. Imaginary roots will
play an essential role in this expression. In particular, the
function
\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}
introduced in Definition
2.5

will turn out not to depend on
a
.

This section is organized as follows. In Subsection

4.1
, if
\mathfrak{h}
is a
\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra and
H

is the corresponding Cartan subgroup, if
\gamma\in H
, we give
explicit formulas for the
determinant of
1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
on various subspaces in terms of a
positive root system.

In Subsection
4.2
, we establish our formula for

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
using the root system.

We use the assumptions and the notation of Section
3
.

4.1.
The determinant of
1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)

Let
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be a
\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra, and let
H\subset G
be the corresponding Cartan subgroup.
Put

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{+}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in R_{+}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{-}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in R_{+}}\mathfrak{g}_{-\alpha}.

By (
3.10
), we get

(4.2)

\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}=\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{+}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{-}.

Let
\gamma\in H
be written as in (
3.57
).
By Theorem
3.18
, we obtain

(4.3)

\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{+}}=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}}\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right).

We write (
4.3
) in the form

(4.4)

\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{+}}=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}}\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}}\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}}\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right).

By the considerations we made in Subsection
3.5
,

-\theta
acts without fixed points on
R^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}
. By
Theorem
3.18
, in the right-hand side of
(
4.4
), the first term is positive, the second is a product
of nonzero real numbers, and the third term is a
product of complex numbers of module
1
.

If
\alpha\in R_{+}
, we choose a square
root
\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)
of

\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)
. In view of the
second identity in (
3.61
), if
\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}
, we may and we will
assume that

(4.5)

\xi_{-\theta\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)=\overline{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)}.

For
\alpha\in R_{+}
, we choose the square root

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)
so that

(4.6)

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)=e^{\left\langle\alpha,a\right\rangle/2}\xi^{1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right).

By (
4.5
), (
4.6
), if
\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}
, then

(4.7)

\xi^{1/2}_{-\theta\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=\overline{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)}.

A square root of

\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{+}}
in (
4.3
)
is given by

(4.8)

\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{+}}^{1/2}=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right).

By proceeding as in (
4.4
), we can rewrite
(
4.8
) in the form

(4.9)

\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{+}}^{1/2}=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}}\xi^{1/2}_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}}\xi^{1/2}_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right).

Using (
4.6
),
(
4.7
), we find that the
first product in the right hand-side of (
4.9
) is
positive, the
second product is either a nonzero real number, or the
product of
\sqrt{-1}
by a nonzero real number, and the third
product is of module
1
.

Definition 4.1
.

Put

(4.10)

\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathrm{sgn}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

Recall that if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}
, then

\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)=\pm 1
.

Proposition 4.2
.

The following identity holds:

(4.11)

\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathrm{sgn}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(a\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

Proof.

If
\alpha\in R\left(a\right)
, by (
3.61
),

\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)
. If
\alpha\notin R\left(\gamma\right)
, by (
3.18
),
\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\neq 1
. By Theorem
3.18
, if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}\left(a\right)\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}\left(\gamma\right)
, we have
\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=-1
, so that
1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)=2
. This completes the proof of our proposition.
∎

Theorem 4.3
.

The following identities hold:

\displaystyle\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(a\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(a\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)^{2},

(4.12)

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}\right|^{1/2}=\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(a\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}_{a}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(a\right)\setminus R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\left(a\right)\setminus R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)^{2}.

Proof.

Using Theorem
3.18
and the third identity in
(
3.19
), we get

(4.13)

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(a\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right),

from which the first equation in (
4.3
) follows.

By proceeding as in Subsection
3.5
, we find that

-\theta
acts on
R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(a\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\right)
without
fixed points, so that
\left|R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(a\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\right)\right|
is even, and so

(4.14)

\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(a\right)\right|}=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)\right|}.

The same arguments also show that

(4.15)

\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(a\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)

is a positive number, and also that

(4.16)

\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(a\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\\
=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(a\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)^{2}.

Moreover, we have the identity of nonzero real numbers,

(4.17)

\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(a\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right)^{2}\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right).

By Theorem
3.18
, if
\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}
, then

(4.18)

\displaystyle\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)=e^{\left\langle\alpha,a\right\rangle}\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)=\pm 1.

Using Proposition

4.2
and (
4.17
), we get

(4.19)

\left|\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(a\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\right|^{1/2}\\
=\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right)e^{\left\langle\alpha,a\right\rangle/2}.

Equation (
4.19
) can be rewritten in the form

(4.20)

\left|\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(a\right)}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\right|^{1/2}\\
=\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right).

By (
4.13
)–(
4.20
), we get the second identity in
(
4.3
).

Since on
\mathfrak{z}_{a}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)
,
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
acts
like
\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)
, the proof of the third identity in (
4.3
) is
the same as the proof of the first identity, which completes the
proof of our theorem.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

By the first two equations in (
4.3
), we deduce that

(4.21)

\mathrm{sgn}\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(a\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(a\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(k^{-1}\right).

Also

(4.22)

\left|R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(a\right)\right|=\dim\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(a\right).

Using (
4.22
), we can rewrite (
4.21
) in the form

(4.23)

\mathrm{sgn}\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\dim\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(k\right)|_{\mathfrak{r}^{\perp}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(a\right)}.

Using Proposition
3.8
, we get

(4.24)

\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(k\right)|_{\mathfrak{r}^{\perp}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(a\right)}=\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(k\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}.

By (
4.23
), (
4.24
), we get

(4.25)

\mathrm{sgn}\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\dim\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}\det\mathrm{Ad}\left(k\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(a\right)},

a result already established in
[
B11
, Proposition 5.4.1]
.

Let

\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}
be the orthogonal space to
\mathfrak{i}
in
\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}
.
By (
2.20
),
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right)
, and by (
3.69
),
\mathfrak{i}\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right)
. Therefore,

(4.26)

\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)\subset\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}.

Similarly, we have the inclusion

(4.27)

R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\subset R_{+}\left(a\right).

Theorem 4.5
.

The following identities hold:

\displaystyle\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}}=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\right)\right|}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)^{2},

(4.28)

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}}\right|^{1/2}=\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right).

Proof.

The proof of the first identity in (
4.5
) is the
same as the proof of the first identity in (
4.3
) that
was given in Theorem
4.3
. Instead of
(
4.14
), we get

(4.29)

\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\right)\right|}=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}.

If in (
4.15
), we
replace
R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(a\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\right)
by

R_{+}\setminus\left(R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\right)
, the
conclusions remain valid. Similarly, (
4.17
),
(
4.19
) remain valid when replacing

R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(a\right)

by
R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(\gamma\right)
. This completes the proof of
our theorem.
∎

4.2.
Evaluation of the function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}
on

i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}

Let
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be a
\theta
-stable Cartan
subalgebra. Take
\gamma\in H
. Then
\gamma
is semisimple, and

\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. In particular,

\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}\subset\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
,
so that functions defined on
i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
restrict to functions on
i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
.

Recall that the function
\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right),\mathcal{M}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
on

i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
were defined in Definition

2.5
.

Definition 4.6
.

If
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, put

(4.30)

\mathscr{L}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\frac{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}.

Like the function

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
in (
2.68
), the function

\mathscr{L}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
is a smooth
function of
h_{\mathfrak{k}}
, which verifies estimates similar to
(
2.77
).
Exactly the same arguments as in
[
B11
, Section 5.5]
and after
(
2.65
) show that there is
an unambiguously defined square root

(4.31)

\mathscr{M}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\left[\frac{1}{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}\mathscr{L}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{1/2}.

This square root is positive for
h_{\mathfrak{k}}=0
.

Theorem 4.7
.

If
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, then

(4.32)

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)}\right)}{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\right)}=\frac{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(k\right)}\right)}{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(k\right)}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\mathscr{L}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

In particular
\mathcal{L}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
does
not depend on
a
.

If
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, we have the
identity,

(4.33)

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\frac{1}{\left|\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}}\right|^{1/2}}\frac{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(k\right)}\right)}{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(k\right)}\right)}\mathscr{M}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

This identity can be written in the form,

(4.34)

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
=\frac{\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)\right|}\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}\left(\gamma\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\xi^{1/2}_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\right)}\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)}\\
\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}.

Proof.

By (
3.28
), we get

(4.35)

\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}=\mathfrak{r}\oplus\mathfrak{c}.

Also
\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}
splits as

(4.36)

\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}=\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}_{\mathfrak{k}}.

By Proposition
3.8
, as
representations of
H\cap K
,
\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}_{\mathfrak{k}}
are equivalent, so that
(
4.32
) holds.

Observe that

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}_{a}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}}>0
, and so
this number has a positive square root. Moreover,

(4.37)

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}}=\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(a\right)}\\
\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{z}_{a}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}}.

By (
2.64
), (
2.65
), (
2.68
),
(
4.32
), and (
4.37
), we get (
4.33
).

Clearly,

(4.38)

\frac{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(k\right)}\right)}{\widehat{A}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(k\right)}\right)}=\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)}.

By proceeding as in the proof of the third identity in (
4.3
),
we get

(4.39)

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)^{2}.

The same argument shows that

(4.40)

\mathscr{L}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\setminus R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|}\\
\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)^{2}}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)^{2}}.

By (
4.39
), (
4.40
), and keeping in mind the fact
that we take the properly positive square root in (
4.31
),
we get

(4.41)

\mathscr{M}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\frac{\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}/R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)\right|}}{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)}\\
\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}.

In the first product in the right-hand side of (
4.41
), we
may as well replace
k^{-1}
by
\gamma
.

By combining the second identity in (
4.5
),
(
4.33
), (
4.38
), and (
4.41
), we get
(
4.34
).
The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

5.
The function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}
when
\gamma
is regular

The purpose of this Section is to study extra properties of the
function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}
when
\gamma
is regular.

This section is organized as follows. In Subsection

5.1
, if
\mathfrak{h}
is a
\theta
-stable Cartan
subalgebra and if
H
is the corresponding Cartan subgroup, if

\gamma\in H
, we describe a neighborhood of
\gamma
in
H
.

In Subsection
5.2
, if
\gamma\in H
, we define the

\gamma
-regular elements in
\mathfrak{h}
, which are such that a
small perturbation of
\gamma
by a
\gamma
-regular element is regular.

In Subsection
5.3
, following Harish-Chandra

[
HC65
]
, we introduce the function

D_{H}
on
H
. This function is an analogue of the
denominator in the Lefschetz formulas.

Finally, in Subsection
5.4
, we specialize the formula obtained
in Theorem
4.7
for
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
to the case where
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
. As a consequence, we prove the unexpected
result that the function

\left(\gamma,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}\times i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}\to\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\in\mathbf{C}
is smooth.

We make the same assumptions and we use the same notation as in Section

4
.

5.1.
A neighborhood of
\gamma
in
H

If
b\in\mathfrak{h}
,
b
splits as

(5.1)

\displaystyle b=b_{\mathfrak{p}}+b_{\mathfrak{k}},

\displaystyle b_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}},\qquad b_{\mathfrak{k}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}.

Put

(5.2)

\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma e^{b}.

Then
\gamma^{\prime}\in H\cap Z\left(\gamma\right)
.

Set

(5.3)

\displaystyle a^{\prime}=a+b_{\mathfrak{p}},

\displaystyle k^{\prime}=ke^{-b_{\mathfrak{k}}}.

Then

(5.4)

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime}=e^{a^{\prime}}k^{\prime-1},

\displaystyle a^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}},\qquad k^{\prime}\in H\cap K\left(\gamma\right),\qquad\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{\prime}\right)a^{\prime}=a^{\prime}.

Also
\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)

preserves the splitting
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\oplus\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)
. Since

1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)
is invertible on
\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)
,
we conclude that for
\epsilon>0
small
enough, if
\left|b\right|\leq\epsilon
,

(5.5)

\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right).

Let

H^{\mathrm{reg}}
be the set of regular elements in
H
.
Assume temporarily that
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, i.e.,
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{h}
. By (
5.5
), for

\epsilon>0
small enough if
\left|b\right|\leq\epsilon
,
then

(5.6)

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=\mathfrak{h},

i.e.,
\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, which is a
trivial conclusion. Since
b\in\mathfrak{h}
, we conclude that
\gamma^{\prime}\in Z\left(\gamma\right),\gamma\in Z\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)
. A priori,

Z\left(\gamma\right)
and
Z\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)
may be distinct.
Still we have the obvious identity

(5.7)

Z^{0}\left(\gamma\right)=Z^{0}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=H^{0}.

5.2.
The
\gamma
-regular elements in
\mathfrak{h}

We no longer assume
\gamma
to be regular. By
(
3.19
), we get

(5.8)

\mathfrak{z}_{a}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)_{\mathbf{C}}=\oplus_{\alpha\in R\left(a\right)\setminus R\left(\gamma\right)}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}.

Let

\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{a}
denote the orthogonal space
to
\mathfrak{h}
in
\mathfrak{z}\left(a\right)
. Then we have the
splitting

(5.9)

\mathfrak{h}_{a}^{\perp}=\mathfrak{h}_{a,\mathfrak{p}}^{\perp}\oplus\mathfrak{h}_{a,\mathfrak{k}}^{\perp}.

By
(
3.19
), we get

(5.10)

\mathfrak{h}_{a,\mathbf{C}}^{\perp}=\oplus_{\alpha\in R\left(a\right)}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}.

Definition 5.1
.

An element
h\in\mathfrak{h}
is said to be
\gamma
-regular if for

\alpha\in R\left(\gamma\right)
,
\left\langle\alpha,h\right\rangle\neq 0
.

The
\gamma
-regular elements in
\mathfrak{h}
are exactly the
regular elements in
\mathfrak{h}
viewed as a Cartan
subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. The
\gamma
-regular elements lie in the complement of a finite family of
hyperplanes in
\mathfrak{h}
.

Since
\mathfrak{h}
is a Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, we define the function

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}

on
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}
as in (
3.41
), i.e.,

(5.11)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h\right)=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\left\langle\alpha,h\right\rangle.

Then
h\in\mathfrak{h}
is
\gamma
-regular if and only if

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h\right)\neq 0
.

Now we use the notation of Subsection
5.1
.

Proposition 5.2
.

There
exists
\epsilon>0
such that if
b\in\mathfrak{h}
is
\gamma
-regular, and
\left|b\right|\leq\epsilon
,
if
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma e^{b}
, then
\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Proof.

For
\epsilon>0
small enough, (
5.5
) holds, so that

(5.12)

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\cap\mathfrak{z}\left(e^{b}\right).

By (
3.7
), we get

(5.13)

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\bigoplus\oplus_{\alpha\in R\left(\gamma\right)}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}.

For
\alpha\in R\left(\gamma\right)
,
e^{b}
acts on
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}
by multiplication by
e^{\left\langle\alpha,b\right\rangle}
. For
\epsilon>0
small enough, if
b
is

\gamma
-regular and
\left|b\right|\leq\epsilon
, for

\alpha\in R\left(\gamma\right)
,
e^{\left\langle\alpha,b\right\rangle}\neq 1
. By (
5.12
), (
5.13
),
we conclude that under the given conditions on
b
,
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=\mathfrak{h}
, i.e.,
\gamma^{\prime}
is regular. The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

5.3.
The function
D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)

Here, we follow Harish-Chandra
[
HC65
, Section
19]
.

Definition 5.3
.

If
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, put
9
9

9

In
[
HC65
, Section
18]
, Harish-Chandra
assumes
G
to be acceptable, i.e.,
\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}
is
assumed to be a weight, so that

D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)
can be globally defined. Here, we only
need a local definition of
D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)
, and we do not
need this assumption.

(5.14)

D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

Using (
3.62
) and proceeding as in the proof of Theorem
4.3
, we get

(5.15)

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\right|}D_{H}^{2}\left(\gamma\right).

By (
5.15
), we deduce that if
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
,
then

(5.16)

\left|\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}}\right|=\left|D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)\right|^{2},

so that
D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)\neq 0
.

5.4.
The function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}
when
\gamma
is
regular

In this subsection, we assume that
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, i.e.,

D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)\neq 0
.

By (
4.30
), (
4.31
), we get

(5.17)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\frac{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}}}{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{p}}}},

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\left[\frac{1}{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}}}\mathscr{L}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{1/2}.

By (
4.10
),
\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)
is given
by

(5.18)

\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathrm{sgn}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

The function
\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)
is locally constant on

H^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Theorem 5.4
.

If
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, we have the
identity,

(5.19)

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\frac{1}{\left|\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(\gamma\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{i}^{\perp}}\right|^{1/2}}\mathscr{M}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

This identity can be written in the form,

(5.20)

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\frac{\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\right|}\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)}{D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)}\\
\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}.

The function
\left(\gamma,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}\times i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}\to\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\in\mathbf{C}
is smooth.

Proof.

The first part of our theorem is a trivial consequence of Theorem

4.7
. For
b_{\mathfrak{k}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, for
\left|b_{\mathfrak{k}}\right|
small enough,
we take

(5.21)

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{\prime-1}\right)=e^{\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{k}}/2\right\rangle}\xi^{1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right).

By
(
4.5
), (
5.21
), we deduce that if
\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}
, then

(5.22)

\xi_{-\theta\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{\prime-1}\right)=\overline{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{\prime-1}\right)}.

The stated smoothness is an obvious consequence of
the above formulas. The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

6.
The Harish-Chandra isomorphism

In this section, if
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
is a Cartan
subalgebra, we describe the Harish-Chandra isomorphism of algebras

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}:Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
. Also we explain the action of
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)

on
C^{\infty}\left(X,F\right)
, and we introduce certain semisimple orbital integrals in which

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
appears.

This section is organized as follows. In Subsection

6.1
, we introduce the center of the enveloping
algebra
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

In Subsection
6.2
, we recall some properties of the
complex
Harish-Chandra isomorphism
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}:Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, including some aspects of its construction.

In Subsection
6.3
, we show that there is a real form
of the Harish-Chandra isomorphism

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}:Z\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

In Subsection
6.4
, we recall the relation of the
Harish-Chandra isomorphism to the Duflo isomorphism that was
established in
[
Du70
]
.

In Subsection
6.5
, we consider the case of the
Casimir.

In Subsection
6.6
, we describe the action of

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
on
C^{\infty}\left(X,F\right)
.

Finally, in Subsection
6.7
, we consider the orbital
integrals in which
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
appears.

6.1.
The center of the enveloping algebra

Recall that the enveloping algebra

U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
was
introduced in Subsection
2.3
.
Then
U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
is a
filtered algebra, and the corresponding
\mathrm{Gr}
is just the
algebra of polynomials
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

Note that
\mathfrak{g}
acts by derivations on

U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
.
Recall that

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
is the center of

U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
, i.e., it is the kernel of the above
derivations.

Observe that
G
acts both on the left and on the right on
C^{\infty}\left(G,\mathbf{R}\right)
by the formula

(6.1)

\displaystyle\gamma_{L}s\left(g\right)=s\left(\gamma^{-1}g\right),

\displaystyle\gamma_{R}s\left(g\right)=s\left(g\gamma\right),

and these two actions commute. They are intertwined by
the involution induced by the involution

g\to\sigma g=g^{-1}
.
Let

D_{L}\left(G\right)
be
the Lie algebras of left-invariant real differential operators on

G
. As we saw in Subsection
2.3
,
U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
can be identified with
D_{L}\left(G\right)
. The algebra

D_{L}\left(G\right)
commutes with the left action of
G
.

If

\mathfrak{g}_{-}
is the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
with the negative of the
original Lie bracket, the isomorphism of
\mathfrak{g}

f\to-f
identifies
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{-}
. This isomorphism
is induced by the involution
\sigma
.

Let

U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{-}\right)
be the enveloping algebra
associated with
\mathfrak{g}_{-}
. Then
U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{-}\right)
can be identified with the algebra of right-invariant
real
differential operators

D_{R}\left(G\right)
. This algebra commutes
with the right action of
G
. Also the isomorphism
f\to-f
induces an
identification of
U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
and
U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{-}\right)
. This identification is still induced by
\sigma
.

We equip
U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right),U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{-}\right)
with the antiautomorphism

*
which is just the adjoint
in the classical
L_{2}
sense when identifying
U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right),U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{-}\right)
with
D_{L}\left(G\right),D_{R}\left(G\right)
. This involution extends to a
\mathbf{C}
-linear
involution of
U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right),U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{-,\mathbf{C}}\right)
.

By definition,
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\subset U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
is the subalgebra of
D_{L}\left(G\right)
which commutes with
right multiplication. Equivalently

(6.2)

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)=D_{L}\left(G\right)\cap D_{R}\left(G\right).

Note that
*
induces an automorphism of
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,
which is an involution, and which we still denote
*
.

The isomorphism of
U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
with
U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{-}\right)
which was described before is the one induced by

\sigma
. It induces the obvious
isomorphism of
D_{L}\left(G\right)
with
D_{R}\left(G\right)
.
This way, we obtain an automorphism
\sigma
of
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
, which is also an involution.

Clearly,

(6.3)

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)=Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)_{\mathbf{C}}.

Equivalently,
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
is equipped with a
complex conjugation, and
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
is the algebra
of complex conjugation invariants in
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
. Also
*
and
\sigma
extend to complex automorphisms
of
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
.

6.2.
The complex form of the Harish-Chandra isomorphism

Let
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be a
\theta
-stable Cartan
subalgebra. By
[
Kn86
, Theorem 8.18]
, there is a canonical
Harish-Chandra isomorphism of filtered algebras,

(6.4)

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}:Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right).

We need to describe the
Harish-Chandra isomorphism in more detail. We fix a positive root

R_{+}
as in Subsection
3.5
. Put

(6.5)

\mathscr{P}=\sum_{\alpha\in R_{+}}U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}.

Observe that
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)=U\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, and also that
U\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\subset U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, so that

S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\subset U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
. By
[
Kn86
, Lemma 8.17]
, we get

(6.6)

\displaystyle S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\cap\mathscr{P}=0,

\displaystyle Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\subset S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\oplus\mathscr{P}.

Let
\phi_{1,R_{+}}
be the projection from
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
on
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
.

Recall that
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
is the algebra of
polynomials on
\mathfrak{h}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}
, and that

\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}
is the half sum of the roots in
R_{+}
.
Let
\phi_{2,R_{+}}
be the filtered
automorphism of
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
that is such that
if
f\in S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, if
h^{*}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}
, then

(6.7)

\phi_{2,R_{+}}f\left(h^{*}\right)=f\left(h^{*}-\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right).

The fundamental result of Harish-Chandra
[
HC56
, Lemmas
18-20]
,
[
Kn86
, Theorem
8.18]
is that

\phi_{2,R_{+}}\phi_{1,R_{+}}
maps
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
onto
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, that it induces an isomorphism of filtered algebras
that does not depend on the choice of

R_{+}
. This is exactly the Harish-Chandra isomorphism

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}:Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
.

Now we proceed as in Subsection
3.11
, i.e., we identify
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
with the algebra

D_{I}^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
of holomorphic differential
operators on
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}
with constant complex coefficients which
are
W\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
-invariant. The
same arguments as in Subsection
3.11
show that there is
an algebra

D_{I}^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
of real
differential operators with constant coefficients on
\mathfrak{h}

such that

(6.8)

D^{\cdot}_{I}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)=D^{\cdot}_{I}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)_{\mathbf{C}},

and that
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
can be
identified with
D^{\cdot}_{I}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

We will now use the assumptions and notation of Subsection

3.7
.
Let
C^{\infty,G}\left(G^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right)
denote the
\mathrm{Ad}
-invariant
smooth complex functions on the open set
G^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Let
C^{\infty,W\left(H:G\right)}\left(H^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right)
be the smooth

W\left(H:G\right)
-invariant functions on
H^{\mathrm{reg}}
. There
is a restriction map

r:C^{\infty,G}\left(G^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right)\to C^{\infty,W\left(H:G\right)}\left(H^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right).

Observe that
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
acts on

C^{\infty,G}\left(G^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right)
, and

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
acts on

C^{\infty,W\left(H:G\right)}\left(H^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right)
.

Let
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
. By
[
HC65
, Lemma
13]
,
[
Kn86
, Theorem 10.33]
, if
f\in C^{\infty,G}\left(G^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right)
, on
H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, we have
the identity

(6.9)

rLf=\frac{1}{D_{H}}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)D_{H}rf.

6.3.
The real form of the Harish-Chandra isomorphism

The involution
h\to-h
induces an involution of

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)\simeq D_{I}^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
. If
N
counts the degree in

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, this involution is just

\left(-1\right)^{N}
. We
still denote this involution by
*
.

In Proposition
3.21
, we proved that
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
is preserved by complex conjugation.
At the end of Subsection
6.1
,
we proved that
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
is also
preserved by complex conjugation. Observe that
\theta
acts on

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right),I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
and preserves
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right),I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

Theorem 6.1
.

If
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, then

(6.10)

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\left(L^{*}\right)=\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*},

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\left(\overline{L}\right)=\overline{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\left(L\right)},\qquad\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\theta L=\theta\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L.

On
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, the involutions

\sigma
and
*
coincide. Finally,
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}
induces an isomorphism
of real filtered algebras:

(6.11)

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right).

Proof.

The first equation in (
6.10
) was established by Harish-Chandra
[
HC56
, Lemma
20]
. For the proof of the next two equations, we will
follow Harish-Chandra, and use the notation in
Subsection
6.2
.

Observe that
\overline{R}_{+}
is also a positive root system. More
precisely, by (
3.35
), we get

(6.12)

\overline{R}_{+}=\overline{R}^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\cup\overline{R}^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\cup\overline{R}^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}.

From the properties of
R_{+}
, (
6.12
) can be rewritten in the form

(6.13)

\overline{R}_{+}=\left(-R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}.

Let
\overline{\mathscr{P}}
denote the conjugate of
\mathscr{P}
in

U\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
. By (
3.8
),

\overline{\mathscr{P}}
is just the object defined in
(
6.5
) associated with
\overline{R}_{+}
.
We deduce that if
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, we have the
identity in
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
,

(6.14)

\phi_{1,\overline{R}_{+}}\overline{L}=\overline{\phi_{1,R_{+}}L}.

Also
\overline{\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}^{*}
is the
half sum of the roots in
\overline{R}_{+}
. By (
6.13
), if

f\in S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, then

(6.15)

\phi_{2,\overline{R}_{+}}\overline{f}=\overline{\phi_{2,R_{+}}f}.

By (
6.14
), (
6.15
), we get the identity in

S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
,

(6.16)

\phi_{2,\overline{R}_{+}}\phi_{1,\overline{R}_{+}}\overline{L}=\overline{\phi_{2,R_{+}}\phi_{1,R_{+}}L}.

Let us now use Harish-Chandra’s result described after (
6.7
). For
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, we can rewrite (
6.16
) as
an identity in
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
,

(6.17)

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\overline{L}=\overline{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L}.

But Harish-Chandra gives more, namely that the image of

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}
is exactly
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
. By (
6.17
),
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
is
preserved by complex conjugation, which we already knew by
Proposition
3.21
, and we also obtain the second equation
in (
6.10
), and (
6.11
).

Also
\theta R_{+}=-\overline{R}_{+}

is a positive root system, and the corresponding half-sum of roots is
given by
\theta\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}
. As in (
6.14
), if

L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, then

(6.18)

\phi_{1,\theta R_{+}}\theta L=\theta\phi_{1,R_{+}}L.

If
f\in S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, then

(6.19)

\phi_{2,\theta R_{+}}\theta f=\theta\phi_{2,R_{+}}f.

By (
6.18
), (
6.19
), we conclude that

(6.20)

\phi_{2,\theta R_{+}}\phi_{1,\theta R_{+}}\theta L=\theta\phi_{2,R_{+}}\phi_{1,R_{+}}L.

Using again the result of Harish-Chandra, from (
6.20
), we
obtain the third equation in (
6.10
).

If
f,h\in C^{\infty,c}\left(G,\mathbf{R}\right)
, the convolution

f*h\in C^{\infty,c}\left(G,\mathbf{R}\right)
is defined by the formula,

(6.21)

f*h\left(g\right)=\int_{G}f\left(g^{-1}g^{\prime}\right)h\left(g^{\prime}\right)dg^{\prime}.

If
A\in D_{R}\left(G\right),B\in D_{L}\left(G\right)
, we get easily

(6.22)

\displaystyle f*Ah=A\left(f*h\right),

\displaystyle f*Bh=\left(B^{*}f\right)*h,\,\,B\left(f*h\right)=\left(\left(\sigma B\right)f\right)*h.

By (
6.2
), (
6.22
), we conclude that if
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, then

(6.23)

\left(L^{*}f\right)*h=\left(\left(\sigma L\right)f\right)*h.

from which we get

(6.24)

L^{*}f=\sigma Lf.

This completes the proof of our theorem.
∎

6.4.
The Duflo and the Harish-Chandra isomorphisms

Here,

S^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
denotes the algebra of
formal power series
\alpha=\sum_{i=0}^{+\infty}\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i}\in S^{i}\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]
. Then

\mathfrak{g}
still acts on
S^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
as an algebra of derivations. Let

I^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
be the subalgebra of
invariant elements in
S^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
.

As in Subsection
3.1
,
S^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
can be identified with the algebra

D^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
of formal real partial
differential operators with constant coefficients on
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, and
I^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
with the
algebra of formal real invariant differential operators with constant
coefficients

D_{I}^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
, which acts
on
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

Then
\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(\cdot\right)\right)\in I^{\cdot}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
.
In the sequel, we view

\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(\cdot\right)\right)
as an element
of
D^{\cdot}_{I}\left[\left[\mathfrak{g}^{*}\right]\right]
.

Let

\tau_{\mathrm{PBW}}
be the Poincaré-Birkhoff-Witt isomorphism of
filtered vector spaces
S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
. Then
\tau_{\mathrm{PBW}}
induces an identification of filtered
vector spaces
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

Definition 6.2
.

Put

(6.25)

\tau_{\mathrm{D}}=\tau_{\mathrm{PBW}}\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(\cdot\right)\right):S^{\cdot}(\mathfrak{g})\to U(\mathfrak{g}).

Then
\tau_{\mathrm{D}}
is an isomorphism of filtered vector spaces,
which commutes with
\theta
.

A result by Duflo
[
Du70
, Théorème V.2]
asserts that
when restricted to
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,

\tau_{\mathrm{D}}
induces an isomorphism of filtered algebras,

(6.26)

I^{\cdot}(\mathfrak{g})\simeq Z(\mathfrak{g}).

By
[
Du70
, Lemme V.1]
, we have the commutative diagram

(6.27)

and the morphisms in (

) commute with
