.

6.5.
The case of the Casimir

Note that
B^{*}|_{\mathfrak{h}}\in I^{2}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
corresponds to the Laplacian

\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}
on
\mathfrak{h}
associated with

B|_{\mathfrak{h}}
.
The following result of Harish-Chandra is established in
[
Kn02
, Example
5.64]
as a consequence of the constructions in Subsection

6.2
.

Proposition 6.3
.

We have the identity:

(6.29)

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}=-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{g}},\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right).

Proposition 6.4
.

The following identity holds:

(6.30)

\tau_{\mathrm{D}}^{-1}C^{\mathfrak{g}}=-B^{*}+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{g}},\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right).

Proof.

Clearly,

(6.31)

\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(x\right)=1+\frac{1}{24}x^{2}+\ldots

Also
B^{*}\in I^{2}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
. Let

e_{1}\ldots,e_{m+n}
be a basis of
\mathfrak{g}
, and let

e^{*}_{1},\ldots,e^{*}_{m+n}
be the basis of
\mathfrak{g}
which is
dual with respect to
B
. By (
6.31
),
we get

(6.32)

\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(\cdot\right)\right)B^{*}=B^{*}+\frac{1}{24}\mathrm{Tr}^{\mathfrak{g}}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(e_{i}\right)\mathrm{ad}\left(e_{j}\right)\right]B^{*}\left(e_{i}^{*},e_{j}^{*}\right).

Equation (
6.32
) can be written in the form

(6.33)

\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(\cdot\right)\right)B^{*}=B^{*}-\frac{1}{24}\mathrm{Tr}^{\mathfrak{g}}\left[C^{\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}}\right].

By (
2.46
), we can rewrite (
6.33
) in the form

(6.34)

\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(\cdot\right)\right)B^{*}=B^{*}+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{g}},\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right).

By (
6.25
), (
6.34
), we get

(6.35)

\tau_{\mathrm{D}}B^{*}=-C^{\mathfrak{g}}+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{g}},\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right),

which is equivalent to (
6.30
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

Remark 6.5
.

Using (
6.28
), Propositions
6.3
and

6.4
can be derived from each other.

6.6.
The action of
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
on
C^{\infty}\left(X,F\right)

Note that
G
acts on the left on
C^{\infty}\left(G,E\right)
as in
(
6.1
), and there is a corresponding action of

D_{R}\left(G\right)
. Also
K
acts on
C^{\infty}\left(G,E\right)
by the formula

(6.36)

k_{R}s\left(g\right)=\rho^{E}\left(k\right)s\left(gk\right),

and this action of
K
commutes with the left action of
G
.
Moreover, we have the identity

(6.37)

C^{\infty}\left(X,F\right)=\left[C^{\infty}\left(G,E\right)\right]^{K}.

Then
D_{R}\left(G\right)
commutes with the action of
K
on

C^{\infty}\left(G,E\right)
. As a subalgebra of

D_{R}\left(G\right)
,
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
also acts on

C^{\infty}\left(G,E\right)
, and its action
commutes with the left action of
G
and with the right action of

K
. The action of
D_{R}\left(G\right)
descends to
C^{\infty}\left(X,F\right)
, so that the action of
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
descends to

C^{\infty}\left(X,F\right)
and commutes with the left action of
G
.

6.7.
The semisimple orbital integrals involving

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)

Let

\mathscr{S}
be the algebra of differential operators acting on

C^{\infty}\left(X,F\right)
with uniformly bounded coefficients
together with their derivatives of any order.
10
10

10

These are
linear combinations of operators
\nabla^{F}_{U_{1}}\ldots\nabla^{F}_{U_{k}}
, where
U_{1},\ldots,U_{k}
are smooth bounded vector fields
with uniformly bounded covariant derivatives of any order.

Definition 6.6
.

Let

C^{\infty,b}\left(X,F\right)
be the vector space of smooth
sections of
F
on
X
which are bounded together with their
covariant
derivatives of any order.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be the space of smooth kernels

Q\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)|_{x,x^{\prime}\in X}
acting on
C^{\infty,b}\left(X,F\right)
and commuting with the
left action of
G
such that there exists
C>0
, and for any

S,S^{\prime}\in\mathscr{S}
, there exists
C_{S,S^{\prime}}>0
for which

(6.38)

\left|SQS^{\prime}\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)\right|\leq C_{S,S^{\prime}}\exp\left(-Cd^{2}\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)\right).

The same arguments as in
[
B11
, Proposition 4.1.2]
shows
that the vector space
\mathcal{Q}
is an algebra with respect to the
composition of operators.

In particular
D_{R}\left(G\right)
commutes with
Q
, and so

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
commutes with
Q
.

Proposition 6.7
.

If
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right),Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, then

LQ\in\mathcal{Q}
.

Proof.

Since
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,
L
commutes with the
left action of
G
, and so
LQ
commutes with this action of
G
.
We fix
x_{0}=p1\in G
. Since
LQ
commutes with
G
, and
G

acts isometrically on
X
, to
establish (
6.38
) for
LQ
, we may as well take

x=x_{0}
. If
U\in\mathfrak{g}
, and if
U^{X}
is the
corresponding vector field on
X
, since
U^{X}
is a Jacobi
field along the geodesics in
X
, there exist
C>0,c>0
such
that

(6.39)

\left|U^{X}\left(x\right)\right|\leq C\exp\left(cd\left(x_{0},x\right)\right).

The above estimate is also valid for the corresponding covariant
derivatives. From (
6.38
), we get the estimate (
6.38
)
for
LQ
when
x=x_{0}
. The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

Let
\gamma\in G
be semisimple. By (
2.58
),
(
6.38
), we have the analogue of (
2.59
), i.e., if

f\in\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)
, then

(6.40)

\left|Q\left(\gamma^{-1}e^{f}x_{0},e^{f}x_{0}\right)\right|\leq C_{\gamma}\exp\left(-c_{\gamma}\left|f\right|^{2}\right).

In
[
B11
, Definition 4.2.2]
, if
\gamma\in G
is semisimple,
if
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, the
orbital integral
\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[Q\right]
is
defined by a formula similar to (
2.60
), i.e.,

(6.41)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[Q\right]=\int_{\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma_{*}Q\left(\gamma^{-1}e^{f}x_{0},e^{f}x_{0}\right)\right]r\left(f\right)df.

The estimates (
2.57
), (
6.40
) guarantees that the
integral in (
6.41
) is well-defined.

By Proposition

6.7
, if
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,
LQ\in\mathcal{Q}
, and so
\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[LQ\right]
is
also well-defined.

7.
The center of
U\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
and the regular
orbital integrals

The purpose of this Section is to evaluate the orbital integrals
for kernels of the
form
L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)
associated with
regular elements in
G
, when
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
. To
establish our formula, we will use the main result of
[
B11
]

described in Theorem
2.9
, the smoothness properties of the function

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
that were obtained
in Section
5
, and the Harish-Chandra
isomorphism in the form given in equation (
6.9
).

This section is organized as follows. In Subsection

7.1
, we recall the classical result of Harish-Chandra
[
HC57b
, Theorem
3]
,
[
HC66
, Section 18]
that expresses
certain orbital integrals on
H^{\mathrm{reg}}
via the action of

Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
on
the orbital integral as a function of
\gamma
.

In Subsection
7.2
, using Theorem
2.9
, we
obtain our formula.

7.1.
The algebra
Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
and the regular orbital
integrals

Let
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right),Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, by
Proposition
6.7
,
LQ\in\mathcal{Q}
. If
f\in C^{\infty}\left(G^{\mathrm{reg}},\mathbf{C}\right)
,
Lf
is a smooth function on

G^{\mathrm{reg}}
. For greater clarity, this function will be
denoted instead
L_{\gamma}f
.

Now we give another proof of a result of Harish-Chandra

[
HC57b
, Theorem 3]
,
[
HC66
, Section 18]
,
[
Kn86
, Proposition
11.9]
.

Proposition 7.1
.

If
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
, the map
\gamma\in G^{\mathrm{reg}}\to\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[Q\right]
is
smooth. If
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
, we have the identity of smooth functions on

G^{\mathrm{reg}}
:

(7.1)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[LQ\right]=\left(\sigma L\right)_{\gamma}\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[Q\right].

Proof.

The map
\left(\gamma,g\right)\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}\times G/H\to g^{-1}\gamma g\in G^{\mathrm{reg}}
is locally a
diffeomorphism. Since
\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[Q\right]

is invariant by conjugation, to obtain the required smoothness,
it is enough to prove that
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}\to\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[Q\right]\in\mathbf{C}

is smooth.

Put

(7.2)

K^{0}\left(H\right)=H^{0}\cap K.

As we saw in Subsection
2.2
,
K^{0}\left(H\right)

is a maximal compact subgroup of
H^{0}
.

Using the notation in
Subsection
5.1
, if
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, and if
\gamma^{\prime}\in H
is close to
\gamma
,
then
\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
and (
5.7
) holds.
Using equation
(
2.31
) in Theorem
2.2
, we deduce that

(7.3)

X\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=H^{0}/K^{0}\left(H\right).

In particular
X\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)
does not depend on
\gamma^{\prime}
,
and
\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\perp}
.
By
(
6.41
), we get

(7.4)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma^{\prime}\right]}\left[Q\right]=\int_{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\perp}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{\prime}_{*}Q\left(\gamma^{\prime-1}e^{f}x_{0},e^{f}x_{0}\right)\right]r\left(f\right)df.

For
\gamma^{\prime}\in H
close enough to
\gamma\in H
, it is elementary to make
the estimate in
[
B11
, Theorem 3.4.1 ]
, which was explained
in (
2.58
), uniform, so that there exists
C>0
such that if

\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
is close enough to
\gamma
, if
f\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\perp},\left|f\right|\geq 1
,

(7.5)

d_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(e^{f}x_{0}\right)\geq C\left|f\right|.

By combining (
6.38
) with
S=1,S^{\prime}=1
and (
7.5
),
for
\gamma^{\prime}\in H
close enough to
\gamma
, there exist
C>0,c>0

such that if
f\in\mathfrak{h}^{\perp}_{\mathfrak{p}},\left|f\right|\geq 1
, we get

(7.6)

\left|\gamma_{*}Q\left(\gamma^{-1}e^{f}x_{0},e^{f}x_{0}\right)\right|\leq C\exp\left(-c\left|f\right|^{2}\right).

Using dominated convergence, by (
2.57
), (
7.6
),
we deduce that
\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[Q\right]
is a
continuous function of
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
.

Let us now prove the above function is smooth on
H^{\mathrm{reg}}
.
The argument is essentially the same as before, by combining the
estimates in (
6.38
) with
S
arbitrary, together with
uniform estimates given in (
6.39
).

Equation (
7.1
) just reflects the fact that
\sigma L

is the image of
L
by the map
g\to g^{-1}
.
∎

7.2.
A geometric formula for the regular orbital integrals

In the sequel we take the function
\mu\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{even}}\left(\mathbf{R}\right)
as in Subsection
2.5
. Let

A\in\mathbf{R},L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

Put

(7.7)

\mathfrak{h}_{i}=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}.

Recall that
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\in D_{I}^{{}^{\cdot}}\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)
. This differential operator acts on smooth functions on

\mathfrak{h}
, but as explained in Subsection
3.1
, it
also acts on smooth functions on
\mathfrak{h}_{i}
.

In the next statement, the smooth kernel
\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}+A}\right)
on
\mathfrak{h}_{i}
acts on the distribution

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}.

Theorem 7.2
.

The following identity holds on
H^{\mathrm{reg}}
:

(7.8)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\\
=\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}+A}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}\right]\left(0\right).

Proof.

If
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, then
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{h}
, and
\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
. When
L=1
, our theorem is
just Theorem
2.9
combined with Proposition

6.3
. When
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
is arbitrary, we use equation (
6.9
) and Proposition

7.1
. Here
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\sigma L
is a differential
operator on
\mathfrak{h}
. We find that on
H^{\mathrm{reg}}
,

(7.9)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\\
=\frac{1}{D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\sigma L\right)\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)r\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\right].

By Theorem
6.1
, we get

(7.10)

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}\sigma L=\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*}.

We combine Theorem
2.9
and equations (
6.29
),
(
7.9
), and
(
7.10
). We get

(7.11)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]=\frac{1}{D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*}\\
\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)r\mu\left(\sqrt{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}+A}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}\right]\left(0\right)\right].

For
greater clarity, we fix
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, and for

b\in\mathfrak{h}
with
\left|b\right|
small enough, we
take
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma e^{b}
as in (
5.2
), so that the
differential operator
\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*}

acts on the variable
b\in\mathfrak{h}
. This action will be
now be denoted
\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*}_{b}
. Also we use the
notation of Subsection
5.1
.

By equation (
5.3
) and equation (
5.20
) in Theorem
5.4
,
and we get

(7.12)

D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\right|}\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{re}}}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\\
\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}.

By the same argument as in (
5.21
), (
5.22
),
if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}
, we can choose

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)
so that
for
\left|b_{\mathfrak{k}}\right|
small enough,

(7.13)

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)=\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right),

i.e., (
7.13
) does not depend on
b
. Similarly,

\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)
is locally constant on

H^{\mathrm{reg}}
. Therefore, the product of these terms in the
right hand-side of (
7.12
) is unaffected by the
action of

\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*}_{b}
in the right-hand side of
(
7.11
).

Also we have the identity,

(7.14)

\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{\prime-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]=\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right].

The right-hand sides of (
7.12
) and (
7.14
)
depend on
b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}
. When only considering
the action of
\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*}_{b}
in the variable

b_{\mathfrak{k}}
, this action
can instead be transferred to the variable
h_{\mathfrak{k}}
with a correcting sign. This argument still does not take into account the fact that

\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)^{*}_{b}
also acts in the variable
b_{\mathfrak{p}}
. However,
differentiating a smooth kernel at the terminal point is equivalent to
compose the smooth kernel with the same change of signs as before. By
combining (
7.11
)–(
7.14
), this
ultimately explains the disappearance of
*
, and to equation
(
7.8
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

8.
The function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}
and the limit of regular orbital integrals

In this section, we verify the compatibility of our formula for
regular orbital integrals of Theorem

7.2
with the limit theorems obtained by Harish-Chandra
for such orbital integrals. Key properties of the function

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}
play a key role in the proofs.

This section is organized as follows. In Subsection

8.1
, given
\gamma\in H
not necessarily regular, we
study the function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
for
\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
close to
\gamma
.

In Subsection
8.2
, if
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,
we define the proper image
L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\in D_{I}^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
.

In Subsection
8.3
, using a formula by Rossmann, we
express a smooth kernel involving
\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}
as the
restriction of another kernel for
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
.

Finally, in Subsection
8.4
, we compute the limit of orbit
integrals as
\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
converges to
\gamma\in H
.

8.1.
The function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}
when
\gamma
is not
regular

Let
\gamma\in G
be a semisimple element as in
(
2.19
). Then
Z^{0}\left(\gamma\right)\subset G
is a
reductive Lie group. Let
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
be a
\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. As we saw in Proposition
3.17
,

\mathfrak{h}
is also a Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. Let
H\subset G
be the corresponding Cartan
subgroup. Recall that the function

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}
on
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}
was introduced in (
5.11
).

Definition 8.1
.

An element
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is said to be

\gamma\,\mathrm{im}
-regular if for
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)
,
\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\neq 0
.

The vanishing locus of an imaginary root being a hyperplane in
i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, the set of

\gamma\,\mathrm{im}
-regular
elements has full Lebesgue measure.

We use the conventions of Section
4
, where we explained
in particular how to choose the

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)|_{\alpha\in R_{+}}
. We extend the definition of
D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)
for
\gamma\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
in Definition
5.3
to general elements
\gamma\in H
by the formula

(8.1)

D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\setminus R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(\gamma\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

By Theorem
3.18
, if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)
, then

\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)=1
, and

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)=\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)=\pm 1
,
so that
\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}\xi^{1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)
is equal to
\pm 1
.

If
\mathfrak{h}
is a
\theta
-stable fundamental Cartan
subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, we will use the
notation introduced in Subsection
3.6
, except that

\mathfrak{g}
is now replaced by
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
,
and the pair
\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\right)
is
replaced by the pair
\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
. Let
R\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
denote the associated root
system. Let
R_{+}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
denote a positive root system. As in
(
3.46
), if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, set

(8.2)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\prod_{\beta\in R_{+}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)}\left\langle\beta,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle.

By (
3.50
), we get

(8.3)

\left[\pi^{h_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}=\left(-1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}\left|R^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\left[\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right]^{2}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle.

We no longer assume
\mathfrak{h}
to be fundamental in
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
.

In the sequel, we use the notation of Subsection
5.1
.
In particular
\gamma\in H
is fixed, and for
b\in\mathfrak{h}
,

\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma e^{b}
. In particular, equations (
5.3
),
(
5.4
) hold.

We establish the following important result.

Theorem 8.2
.

For
\epsilon>0
small enough, there exist
c>0,C>0
such that for
b\in\mathfrak{h}

\gamma
-regular with
\left|b\right|\leq\epsilon,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, then

(8.4)

\left|\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right|\leq C\exp\left(c\left|h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right|\right).

If
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is not

\gamma\,\mathrm{im}
-regular, the left-hand side of (
8.4
)
vanishes.

If
\mathfrak{h}
is not the fundamental Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, for
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, as
b\in\mathfrak{h}

\gamma
-regular tends to
0
,

(8.5)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\to 0.

If
\mathfrak{h}
is the fundamental Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is
\gamma\,\mathrm{im}
-regular, as

b\in\mathfrak{h}

\gamma
-regular tends to
0
,

(8.6)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\to\left(-1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}\left|R^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|+\left|R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|}\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}\\
\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

Proof.

By (
5.11
), we get

(8.7)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle.

We can rewrite (
8.7
), in the form

(8.8)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)}\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{p}}\right\rangle\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\prod_{\alpha\in R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\gamma\right)}\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle.

By (
8.8
), we deduce that if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is not
\gamma\,\mathrm{im}
-regular,
(
8.8
) vanishes.

If
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)
, then

\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)=1
, so that

\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)=\pm 1
. Since
\alpha\in R\left(\gamma\right)
,
if
b\in\mathfrak{h}
is

\gamma
-regular, then
\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\neq 0
. If
\epsilon>0
is small enough, if
b\in\mathfrak{h}
is
\gamma
-regular, and
\left|b_{\mathfrak{k}}\right|\leq\epsilon
, if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, then
1-e^{-\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}\neq 0
,
so that the following expression is well-defined,

(8.9)

\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}\\
=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}{1-e^{-\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}}\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right).

Equation (
8.9
) can be rewritten in the form

(8.10)

\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}\\
=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle/2}{\sinh\left(\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle/2\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right).

When
h_{\mathfrak{k}}
is
\gamma

\mathrm{im}
-regular, we have an
identity similar to (
8.10
),

(8.11)

\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}\\
=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\sinh\left(\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle/2\right)}{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle/2}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right).

If
x\in\mathbf{R},y\in\mathbf{R}
,

(8.12)

\left|1-e^{x+iy}\right|\geq\left|1-e^{x}\right|.

By (
8.12
), we deduce that

(8.13)

\left|\sinh\left(\left(x+iy\right)/2\right)\right|\geq\left|\sinh\left(x/2\right)\right|.

By (
8.13
), there exists
C>0
such that if
e^{x+iy}\neq 1
,

(8.14)

\left|\frac{x}{\sinh\left(\left(x+iy\right)/2\right)}\right|\leq C.

By (
8.10
), (
8.14
), if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is
\gamma

\mathrm{im}
-regular, we get

(8.15)

\left|\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}\right|\leq C.

The
\gamma
-regularity of
b
does not play any role in the above
estimate.

If
\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)
, then

\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}\right)\neq 1
, so that for
\epsilon>0

small enough, if
\left|b_{\mathfrak{k}}\right|\leq\epsilon
, the complex numbers

\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)
which have
module
1
, stay away from
1
. Given
\eta\in\left]0,\pi\right[
,
there is
C_{\eta}>0
such that
if
x\in\mathbf{R}_{+},y\in\left[\eta,2\pi-\eta\right]
, then

(8.16)

\left|xe^{iy}-x^{-1}e^{-iy}\right|\geq C_{\eta}.

By (
8.16
), we deduce that for

\epsilon>0
small enough, if

\left|b_{\mathfrak{k}}\right|\leq\epsilon
, and
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, then

(8.17)

\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{1}{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}\\
=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}{1-e^{-\left\langle\alpha,b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(k^{-1}\right)}

is well-defined, and moreover,

(8.18)

\left|\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{1}{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}\right|\leq C.

By combining (
5.20
), (
8.8
), (
8.15
),
and (
8.18
), we get (
8.4
), and also the fact
that if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is not

\gamma

\mathrm{im}
-regular, the
left-hand side of (
8.4
) vanishes.

By Proposition
3.7
,
\mathfrak{h}
is the fundamental
Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
if and only if

R^{\mathrm{re}}\left(\gamma\right)
is empty.

If
\mathfrak{h}
is not the fundamental Cartan
subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
,

R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)
is nonempty. Using
(
5.20
),
(
8.8
) and the previous bounds, we get (
8.5
).

From now on, we assume that
\mathfrak{h}
is the fundamental Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. By (
4.10
), since

R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)
is empty, we get

(8.19)

\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathrm{sgn}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}}\left(1-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

For
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}
, then

\xi_{\alpha}\left(\gamma\right)\neq 1
so that for

\epsilon>0
small enough, if
\left|b_{\mathfrak{p}}\right|\leq\epsilon
,

(8.20)

\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right).

Also if
\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}
,

\xi_{\alpha}\left(k^{\prime}\right)=\xi_{\alpha}\left(k\right)
. By
the above, it follows that for
b\in\mathfrak{h}

\gamma
-regular close enough to
0
,

(8.21)

\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{\prime-1}\right)=\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right).

By (
8.10
), (
8.11
), and (
8.14
),
if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is
\gamma
im-regular, if
b\in\mathfrak{h}

\gamma
-regular tends to
0
,

\displaystyle\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}

(8.22)

\displaystyle\qquad\to\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi_{\alpha}^{-1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)}{\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad\to\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\frac{\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)}{\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)}.

By (
5.20
), by the considerations after
(
8.1
), by (
8.3
), (
8.8
), (
8.21
),
and (
8.1
), we deduce that if

h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is

\gamma
im-regular, then

(8.23)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\to\epsilon_{D}\left(\gamma\right)\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\right|+\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\\
\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{re}}_{+}\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)}\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\widehat{A}\left(\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle\right)}\\
\frac{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}{\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\setminus R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left(\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)-\xi^{-1/2}_{\alpha}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)}.

By comparing (
4.34
) and the right-hand side of
(
8.23
), we get (
8.6
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

8.2.
The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
and the
isomorphisms of Harish-Chandra and Duflo

Here, we will use results contained in Subsections
3.11
,

6.1
, and
6.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a

\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
.
We have the Harish-Chandra and Duflo isomorphisms of filtered
algebras:

(8.24)

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}:Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right),

\displaystyle\tau_{\mathrm{D}}:I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right).

As explained in Subsection
6.4
, the above isomorphisms
are compatible.

By (
3.78
), we have the isomorphisms,

(8.25)

\displaystyle r:I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right),

\displaystyle r:I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

Recall that we have the splitting

(8.26)

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\oplus\mathfrak{z}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right).

Let

denote the projection

\mathfrak{g}\to\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. This map induces a
corresponding morphism of
\mathbf{Z}
-graded algebras

.
Let

i
be the obvious
morphism
I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)\to I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
.

We have the
commutative diagram

(8.27)

Definition 8.3
.

If
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
, let

be given by

(8.28)

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}=r_{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\tau^{-1}_{\mathrm{D}}\left(L\right).

The map
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\to L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\in I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
is a morphism of filtered algebras.

Proposition 8.4
.

If
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
, the following identity holds:

(8.29)

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}=r^{-1}i\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L.

Proof.

This follows from (
6.28
), (
8.27
), and
(
8.28
).
∎

As we saw in Subsections
3.1
and
3.11
, we have the identification

(8.30)

I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\simeq D_{I}^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right).

In the sequel, when there is no ambiguity, if
L\in Z^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,
L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}

will be considered as an element of
D_{I}^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
.

Proposition 8.5
.

The following identity holds:

(8.31)

\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}=-\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{g}},\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right).

Proof.

By Proposition
6.3
and by (
8.29
), we get
(

).

8.3.
An application of Rossmann’s formula

We know that
a\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Since
a
is in the
center of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, if
\alpha\in R\left(\gamma\right)
, then
\left\langle\alpha,a\right\rangle=0
.
By (
5.11
), we deduce that if
h\in\mathfrak{h}
,

(8.32)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h+a\right)=\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h\right).

We identify
\mathfrak{h}
and
\mathfrak{h}^{*}
via the form
B
, so
that

(8.33)

S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}^{*}\right)\simeq S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}\right).

As we saw in Subsection
3.1
,

S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)
can be identified with the
algebra
D^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)
of differential operators with constant coefficients on

\mathfrak{h}
. Let
\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\in D^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)

denote the differential operator on
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}
associated with
\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\in S^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
.

Recall that

\mathfrak{h}_{i}
was defined in (
7.7
). Let
\mu\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{even}}\left(\mathbf{R}\right)
be taken as
in Subsection
2.5
. If
A\in\mathbf{R}
, let
\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+A}\right)\left(h\right)
be the smooth convolution kernel on
\mathfrak{h}_{i}
associated
with the operator
\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+A}\right)
. If
f\in C^{\infty,c}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathbf{R}\right)
, then

(8.34)

\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+A}\right)f\left(h\right)=\int_{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}}\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+A}\right)\left(h-h^{\prime}\right)f\left(h^{\prime}\right)dh^{\prime}.

We use the corresponding notation on

\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)\oplus i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
.

Here, we use the notation of Subsection
8.2
. In the
sequel,
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L,L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}

are viewed as differential operators in

D^{\cdot}_{I}\left(\mathfrak{h}\right),D^{\cdot}_{I}\left(\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
. These
differential operators can be composed with the above
convolution kernels.

Proposition 8.6
.

We have the identity of smooth functions on
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
,

(8.35)

\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+A}\right)\left(h\right)\\
=\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(-2\pi h\right)L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(h\right).

Proof.

In the proof, we will identify
\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)\oplus i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)

as a real vector space to
\mathfrak{u}\left(\gamma\right)=i\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)\oplus\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
, which is the compact form of

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
.

We identify the real Euclidean vector space
\mathfrak{h}_{i}
to its
dual by its scalar product. Let
\mathcal{F}^{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}
denote the classical Fourier transform on

\mathfrak{h}_{i}
. If
f\in\mathcal{S}\left(\mathfrak{h}_{i}\right)
, if
h^{*}\in\mathfrak{h}_{i}
, then

(8.36)

\mathcal{F}^{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}f\left(h^{*}\right)=\int_{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}\exp\left(-2i\pi B\left(h,h^{*}\right)\right)f\left(h\right)dh.

Put

(8.37)

\breve{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}f\left(h^{*}\right)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}\left(-h^{*}\right).

Then

(8.38)

\mathcal{F}^{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}\left[\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+A}\right)\right]\left(h^{*}\right)\\
=\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(2i\pi h^{*}\right)\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\left(2i\pi h^{*}\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{4\pi^{2}B\left(h^{*},h^{*}\right)+A}\right).

By (
8.38
), we get

(8.39)

\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{-\Delta^{\mathfrak{h}}+A}\right)\left(h\right)\\
=\breve{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(2i\pi h^{*}\right)\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\left(2i\pi h^{*}\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{4\pi^{2}B\left(h^{*},h^{*}\right)+A}\right)\right]\left(h\right).

We can define the Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}^{\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)}
on the Euclidean vector space

\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
, which is canonically
identified to the Lie algebra

\mathfrak{u}\left(\gamma\right)
.
The function
B\left(h^{*},h^{*}\right)
extends to a

\mathrm{Ad}\left(\mathfrak{u}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
-invariant function on
\mathfrak{u}\left(\gamma\right)
. By Proposition
8.4
, the

\mathrm{Ad}\left(\mathfrak{u}\left(\gamma\right)\right)
-invariant function

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}
on
\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
restricts to the
function
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L
on
\mathfrak{h}_{i}
. By Rossmann’s
formula
[
R78
, Theorem p. 209]
,

[
V79
, Theorem p. 13]
, we get

(8.40)

\breve{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathfrak{h}_{i}}\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(2i\pi h^{*}\right)\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\left(2i\pi h^{*}\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{4\pi^{2}B\left(h^{*},h^{*}\right)+A}\right)\right]\left(h\right)\\
=\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(-2\pi h\right)\breve{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)}\left[L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(2i\pi h^{*}\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{4\pi^{2}B\left(h^{*},h^{*}\right)+A}\right)\right]\left(h\right).

By (
8.39
), (
8.40
), we get
(
8.35
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

8.4.
The limit of certain orbital integrals

We use the notation of Subsection
8.1
. As we saw in
Subsection
7.1
,

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma^{\prime}\right]}\left[\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]
is a smooth function of
\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
. Recall that
\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}
is a differential operator on
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathbf{C}}
. To make our formulas clearer, we will denote its action in
the variables
b
or
h
as
\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}_{b}
or
\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}_{h}
.

Proposition 8.7
.

The following identity holds on
H^{\mathrm{reg}}
:

(8.41)

\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma^{\prime}\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\right]\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}_{h}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}+A}\right)\\
\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a^{\prime}}\right]\left(0\right)\\
\\
=\left(-2\pi\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(-a-b_{\mathfrak{p}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

Proof.

As we saw in Theorem
5.4
, the function

\left(\gamma^{\prime},h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}\times i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}\to\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\in\mathbf{C}
is smooth. If
\gamma^{\prime}\in H^{\mathrm{reg}}
, then

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)=\mathfrak{h}
, so that by equation
(
7.8
) in Theorem

7.2
, we get

(8.42)

\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma^{\prime}\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\right]\\
=\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}+A}\right)\\
\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}_{b}\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a^{\prime}}\right]\left(0\right).

In the right hand-side of (
8.42
), the differential
operator

\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}_{b}
acts
on the distribution on the right in the variables
b=\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}},b_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\in\mathfrak{h}
, and
not on the variable
h_{\mathfrak{k}}
. The situation is actually
strictly similar to what we already met in the proofs of Proposition

7.1
and Theorem

7.2
. We obtain this way the first identity in
(
8.41
).

By Proposition
8.6
, using the conventions in
(
8.34
), we get

(8.43)

\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}+A}\right)\\
\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a^{\prime}}\right]\left(0\right)\\
=\left(-2\pi\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(-a-b_{\mathfrak{p}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(a+b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{b_{\mathfrak{k}}-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

By (
8.32
), we get

(8.44)

\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(a+b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)=\pi^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(b_{\mathfrak{p}}+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

By the first identity in (
8.41
) and by
(
8.43
), (
8.44
), we get the second
identity in (
8.41
). The proof of our proposition is completed.
∎

Let

be a maximal torus in

W\left(\mathfrak{t}\left(\gamma\right):\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)

11

We use this

be the corresponding Weyl group.

Theorem 8.8
.

If
\mathfrak{h}

\gamma

\gamma

\to\left(-1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}\left(\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)-\dim\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)}\frac{\left|W\left(\mathfrak{t}\left(\gamma\right):\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\right|}{\mathrm{Vol}\left(K^{0}\left(\gamma\right)/T\left(\gamma\right)\right)}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{+}}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)\\
D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)\left(2\pi\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(-a-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]dY_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}.

Proof.

First, we consider the case where
\mathfrak{h}

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. Using equations (
2.80
),
(
8.4
),
(
8.5
),
(
8.41
), and dominated convergence,
we get (
8.45
).

Assume now that
\mathfrak{h}
is the fundamental Cartan
subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
. Using equations
(
2.80
),
(
8.4
), (
8.6
), (
8.41
), since the

8.6
) takes place except on a Lebesgue
