dominated convergence, so that as

b\in\mathfrak{h}

\gamma
-regular tends to
0
,

(8.47)

\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma^{\prime}\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\right]\\
\to\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|R^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|+\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma\right)\\
\left(2\pi\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(-a-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

The operators
L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}
and
\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}
on
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
are both

K\left(\gamma\right)
-invariant, and so the smooth function

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(-a-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)

on
i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
is also
K\left(\gamma\right)
-invariant. This is also the case for the function

\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right].

Since
\mathfrak{h}
is the fundamental Cartan subalgebra of

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
,
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is a
Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
. By Weyl’s
integration formula, and taking into account the fact that on

i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}
,
\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}
is
nonnegative, we obtain

(8.48)

\int_{i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(-a-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]dY_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\\
=\frac{\mathrm{Vol}\left(K^{0}\left(\gamma\right)/T\left(\gamma\right)\right)}{\left|W\left(\mathfrak{t}\left(\gamma\right):\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left(-a-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}},\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

Using in particular Proposition
3.10
applied to
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, we have the identities,

\displaystyle\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|=\dim\mathfrak{c}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)+\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathfrak{i}\left(k\right),

(8.49)

\displaystyle\left|R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|=\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(k\right),

\displaystyle\left|R^{\mathrm{c}}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|=\dim\mathfrak{c}_{+}\left(\gamma\right).

By (
8.4
), we deduce that

(8.50)

\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|+\left|R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\gamma\right)\right|\\
=\dim\mathfrak{c}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)+\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(k\right)+\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathfrak{i}\left(k\right)+\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathfrak{c}_{+}\left(\gamma\right).

By Proposition
3.10
,
\mathfrak{c}_{+}\left(\gamma\right)

has even dimension. This is also the case for
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{k}}\left(k\right)
. By (
8.50
), we get the equality

\mathrm{mod}\,2
,

(8.51)

\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|+\left|R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\gamma\right)\right|=\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(k\right)+\frac{1}{4}\dim\mathfrak{c}\left(\gamma\right).

By equation (
3.32
) in Proposition
3.8

applied to
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, since
\mathfrak{h}
is
fundamental in
\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, we get

(8.52)

\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)-\dim\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\dim\mathfrak{i}_{\mathfrak{p}}\left(k\right)+\frac{1}{2}\dim\mathfrak{c}\left(\gamma\right).

By (
8.51
), (
8.52
), we get

(8.53)

\left(-1\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|+\left|R_{\mathfrak{k},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)\right|+\frac{1}{2}\left|R_{+}^{\mathrm{c}}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}=\left(-1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}\left(\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)-\dim\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)}.

When
\gamma=1
, the above identity had been established by

[
HC64
, Lemma 18]
.

By (
8.47
), (
8.48
), and (
8.53
), we get
(
8.46
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

9.
The final formula

In this section, we establish our final formula in the case of a
non necessarily regular semisimple element
\gamma\in G
. Our formula
extends both the formula in Theorem
2.9
valid for

\gamma
semisimple
and
L=1
, and the formula in Theorem
7.2
valid for
\gamma
regular.
To establish our main result, we combine a fundamental result of
Harish-Chandra with the results we obtained in Section

8
. Also, along the lines of
[
B11
, Chapter 6]
, we give a wave kernel formulation of our main
result.

This section is organized as follows. In Subsection

9.1
, we establish our main result.

In Subsection
9.2
, as in
[
B11
]
, we reformulate our main result in terms
of wave kernels.

Finally, in Subsection
9.3
, we verify our main formula
is compatible to natural operations on orbital integrals.

9.1.
The general case

Let
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
. Here, we take
\gamma\in G
semisimple as in
(
2.19
).
We will extend Theorem
7.2
to non regular

\gamma
.

Theorem 9.1
.

The following identity holds:

(9.1)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\\
=L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}\right]\left(0\right).

Proof.

By a result of Harish-Chandra
[
HC66
, Lemma 28]
,

[
Va77
, Part II, Section 12.5, Theorem 13]
, we know that if

\mathfrak{h}
is the fundamental Cartan subalgebra in

\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)
, there is a
universal constant
c_{\gamma}
depending only on
\gamma

such that with the notation in
Theorem
8.8
, as
b\in\mathfrak{h}

\gamma
-regular
tends to
0
,

(9.2)

\overline{\pi}^{\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\left[D_{H}\left(\gamma^{\prime}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma^{\prime}\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\right]\\
\to c_{\gamma}\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right].

In Theorem
8.8
, we gave another proof of
the existence of the limit in (
9.2
). Also by the
fundamental result of
[
B11
]
stated as Theorem

2.9
, when
L=1
, the integral in the right-hand side of
(
8.46
) coincides with the orbital integral

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]
.
To identify the constant
c_{\gamma}
, we only need to prove that one
of these last orbital integrals does not vanish. It is enough to
take
E
to be the trivial representation, and

\mu\left(x\right)=\exp\left(-x^{2}\right)
. Since the scalar heat
kernel on
X
is positive, the corresponding orbital integrals do not
vanish. So we find that

(9.3)

c_{\gamma}=\left(-1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}\left(\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)-\dim\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)}\frac{\left|W\left(\mathfrak{t}\left(\gamma\right):\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\right|}{\mathrm{Vol}\left(K^{0}\left(\gamma\right)/T\left(\gamma\right)\right)}\\
\left(2\pi\right)^{\left|R_{+}\left(\gamma\right)\right|}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{+}}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\xi_{\alpha}^{1/2}\left(k^{-1}\right)D_{H}\left(\gamma\right).

When
\gamma=1
, this computation has already been done by
Harish-Chandra in
[
HC75
, Section 37,
Theorem 1]
.
12
12

12

More precisely, if
G=KAN
is the
Iwasawa decomposition, when
\gamma=1
, the constant obtained in
[HC75] is
2^{\frac{\dim N}{2}}c_{\gamma}
. In [HC75, Section 7],
Harish-Chandra uses another normalization for the Haar measure on
G
,
which is adapted to the Iwasawa decomposition. By [HC75, p.202], the
ratio of these two normalizations is given by
2^{\frac{\dim N}{2}}
, which explains the discrepancy.
For the case of a general
\gamma
, this
formula can also be derived from
[
HC66
, p. 34]
and from
the reference given before.

By combining (
8.46
), (
9.2
), and
(
9.3
), we get (
9.1
). The proof of our
theorem is completed.
∎

9.2.
A microlocal version

We still take
\gamma\in G
semisimple as in
(
2.19
).

We will proceed as in
[
B11
, Section 6.3]
, to which we
refer for more details. In the sequel we identify
TX
and
T^{*}X

by the metric.

Let

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]
be the even
distribution on
\mathbf{R}
such that
for any
\mu\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{even}}\left(\mathbf{R}\right)
with

\widehat{\mu}
having compact support,

(9.4)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]=\int_{\mathbf{R}}\widehat{\mu}\left(s\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\cos\left(2\pi s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]ds.

The
operator
L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)
defines a
distribution on
\mathbf{R}\times X\times X
. By finite propagation speed
[
CP81
, section
7.8]
,
[
T81
, section 4.4]
, its
support is included in
\left(s,x,x^{\prime}\right),\left|s\right|\geq d\left(x,x^{\prime}\right)
. Let
\mathcal{X}
be the total
space of
TX
. Let
s\in\mathbf{R}\to\varphi_{s}
be the geodesic
flow on

\mathcal{X}
. Let

\tau
be the variable dual to
s
. By

[
Hö85
, Theorem 23.1.4 and remark]
, the
wave front set

\mathrm{WF}\left(L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right)
of the distribution

L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)
is the conic set in

\mathbf{R}^{2}\times T^{*}X\times T^{*}X
generated by

\left(x^{\prime},-Y^{\prime}\right)=\varphi_{\pm s}\left(x,Y\right),\left|Y\right|=1,\tau=\pm 1
. Conic means that the dilations by

\lambda>0
are applied simultaneously to the variables
Y,Y^{\prime},\tau
.

As explained in
[
B11
, Section 3.4]
, in the geodesic coordinate system centered at
x_{0}=p1
,
\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)
can be identified
with a smooth
submanifold

P^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)
of
X
. Let

N_{P^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)/X}
be the orthogonal bundle to

TP^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)
in
TX
.

Set

(9.5)

\Delta^{\gamma}_{X}=\left\{\left(x,\gamma x\right),x\in P^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)\right\}.

Then
\Delta^{\gamma}_{X}
is a smooth submanifold of
X\times X
.
The
conormal bundle to
\mathbf{R}\times\Delta^{\gamma}_{X}\subset\mathbf{R}\times X\times X
is the set

\left(\left(s,\tau\right),\left(x,Y\right),\left(x^{\prime},Y^{\prime}\right)\right)\in\mathbf{R}^{2}\times\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
such
that
\tau=0,x\in P^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right),x^{\prime}=\gamma x,\gamma^{*}Y^{\prime}+Y\in N_{P^{\perp}\left(\gamma\right)/X}
.

By
[
Hö83
, Theorem 8.2.10]
,

L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\Delta^{\gamma}_{X}

is
a well-defined distribution on
\mathbf{R}\times X\times X
, and its wave
front set is the formal sum of the wave front sets of the two above
distributions.
In particular
the pushforward of the distribution

\mathrm{Tr}^{F}\left[\gamma L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]
by the projection
\mathbf{R}\times X\times X\to\mathbf{R}
is well-defined. It will be denoted

(9.6)

\int_{\Delta^{\gamma}_{X}}\mathrm{Tr}^{F}\left[\gamma L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right].

This is an even distribution on
\mathbf{R}
.

Tautologically, we have the identity of even distributions on
\mathbf{R}
,

(9.7)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]=\int_{\Delta^{\gamma}_{X}}\mathrm{Tr}^{F}\left[\gamma L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right].

We have the result stablished in
[
B11
, Proposition 6.3.1]
.

Proposition 9.2
.

The singular support of

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]

is included in
s=\pm\left|a\right|
, and the ordinary support is
included in

\left\{s\in\mathbf{R},\left|s\right|\geq\left|a\right|\right\}.

If
a=0
, if
\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)=0
, the singular support of

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]

is empty.

We define the even distribution on
\mathbf{R}
,

(9.8)

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\cos\left(s\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}\ e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}\right]\left(0\right)

by the formula

(9.9)

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\mu\left(\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}\right]\left(0\right)\\
=\int_{\mathbf{R}}\widehat{\mu}\left(s\right)L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\cos\left(2\pi s\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\\
\left[\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}\right]\left(0\right).

Let
z=\left(y,Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)
be the generic element of

\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)=\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)\oplus i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)
.
Using finite propagation speed for the wave equation,

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\cos\left(s\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)

is a distribution on
\mathbf{R}\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)

whose support is included in
\left(s,z,z^{\prime}\right),\left|s\right|\geq\left|z^{\prime}-z\right|
. Moreover, by

[
Hö85
, Theorem 23.1.4 and remark]
, its
wave front set is the conic
set associated with

\left(y^{\prime},-Y^{\prime}\right)=\left(y\pm sY,Y\right),\left|Y\right|=1,\tau=\pm 1
. Conic set means again that the dilations
by
\lambda>0
are applied to the variables
Y,Y^{\prime},\tau
.

Set

(9.10)

H^{\gamma}=\left\{0\right\}\times\left(a,i\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)\right)\subset\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right).

The wave front set associated with

\mathbf{R}\times H^{\gamma}\subset\mathbf{R}\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
is such that
Y^{\prime\mathfrak{k}\left(\gamma\right)}=0,\tau=0
,
so that the product

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\cos\left(s\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)H^{\gamma}

is well-defined.

The function
\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]
can be viewed as a
smooth function on the second copy of
\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
in
\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
. It lifts to a smooth
function on
\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
.

Therefore,

(9.11)

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\cos\left(s\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)H^{\gamma}\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]

is a well-defined distribution on
\mathbf{R}\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
. The pushforward
of this distribution by the projection
\mathbf{R}\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\times\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)\to\mathbf{R}
is denoted

(9.12)

\int_{H^{\gamma}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\cos\left(s\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right].

This is an even distribution supported in
\left|s\right|\geq\left|a\right|
, with singular support
included in
s=\pm\left|a\right|
. Note that if
a=0
and if
\mathfrak{p}\left(\gamma\right)=0
, the singular support of this distribution is
empty.

Theorem 9.3
.

We have the identity of even distributions on
\mathbf{R}
supported on

\left|s\right|\geq\left|a\right|
with singular
support included in
\pm\left|a\right|
,

(9.13)

\int_{\Delta^{\gamma}_{X}}\mathrm{Tr}^{F}\left[\gamma L\cos\left(s\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]\\
=\int_{H^{\gamma}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\cos\left(s\sqrt{\left(C^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}+A}\right)\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right].

Proof.

We use Theorem
9.1
, and we proceed as in the
proof of
[
B11
, Theorem 6.3.2]
.
∎

9.3.
Compatibility properties of the formula

Let us give a direct proof that the right-hand side of
(
9.1
) is invariant by conjugation
of
\gamma
in
G
.
Indeed let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
be two conjugate elements in
G
as in
Theorem
2.3
. By this theorem,
they are also
conjugate by an element
k^{\prime\prime}
of
K
, and equation
(
2.33
) holds. Since the character
of the representation
\rho^{E}
is invariant by conjugation by
elements of
K
, the right hand-sides of (
9.1
) associated with

\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
coincide.

We will denote the dependence of our orbital integrals on

E

with an extra superscript
E
. If
L\in Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)
,
by Theorem
6.1
, the
L_{2}
transpose of
L
is
just
\sigma\left(L\right)
. Observe that
C^{\mathfrak{g},X}
is
symmetric, i.e., it is equal to its transpose. Then one has the easy
formula

(9.14)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma^{-1}\right],E^{*}}\left[\sigma\left(L\right)\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]=\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right],E}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right],E}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right]}=\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[\gamma\right],E^{*}}\left[L\mu\left(\sqrt{C^{\mathfrak{g},X}+A}\right)\right].

Using the identities in (
2.71
), we can recover
(
9.14
) from (
9.1
).

Finally, it is easy to verify that, as it should be, our formula is unchanged when
replacing
\gamma,L
by
\theta\gamma,\theta L
.

10.
Orbital integrals and the index theorem

The purpose of this section is to verify the compatibility of our
formula for orbital integrals with the index theorem of Atiyah-Singer

[
AS68a
,
AS68b
]
, to the Lefschetz formulas
of
[
ABo67
,
ABo68
]
for Dirac operators, to the index
formula of Kawasaki
[
K79
]
. More precisely we extend to
the case of an arbitrary
L
what was done in
[
B11
, Chapter
7]
in the case
L=1
. Also we verify the compatibility of
our results with results of Huang-Pandžić
[
HuP02
]

who established the
Vogan conjecture on Dirac cohomology.

This section is organized as follows. In Subsection

10.1
, we construct the Dirac operator
D^{X}
on the
symmetric space
X
.

In Subsection
10.2
, we introduce the relevant notation
when
G
and

K
have the same complex rank.

In Subsection
10.3
, we evaluate the orbital integrals
associated with the index theorem for Dirac operators when
\gamma

semisimple is nonelliptic, and also when
\gamma=1
.

In Subsection
10.4
, when
\gamma
is elliptic, we
consider again the case where the difference of complex ranks is
still equal to
0
.

In Subsection
10.5
, we evaluate the orbital integrals
associated with the index theorem for the Dirac operator.

Finally, in Subsection
10.6
, we verify the
compatibility of our results with the results of Huang-Pandžić
[
HuP02
]
.

10.1.
The Dirac operator on
X

Here, we use the notation of Section
2
. We assume that

K
is simply connected, and also that
\mathfrak{p}
is even-dimensional and oriented. Let
c\left(\mathfrak{p}\right)
be the Clifford algebra associated with

\left(\mathfrak{p},B|_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)
.

As explained in
[
B11
, Section
7.2]
, the representation
\rho^{\mathfrak{p}}:K\to\mathrm{SO}\left(\mathfrak{p}\right)
lifts to a representation
K\to\mathrm{Aut}^{\mathrm{even}}\left(S^{\mathfrak{p}}\right)
, where

S^{\mathfrak{p}}=S^{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}\oplus S^{\mathfrak{p}}_{-}
is
the
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
-graded Hermitian vector space of
\mathfrak{p}
-spinors.
We have the identification of
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
-graded algebras,

(10.1)

c\left(\mathfrak{p}\right)\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{C}=\mathrm{End}\left(S^{\mathfrak{p}}\right).

Set

(10.2)

S^{TX}=G\times_{K}S^{\mathfrak{p}}.

The
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
-graded vector bundle
S^{TX}
inherits a
unitary connection
\nabla^{S^{TX}}
.

Let
\nabla^{S^{TX}\otimes F}
be
the connection on
S^{TX}\otimes F
associated with
\nabla^{S^{TX}},\nabla^{F}
.

Recall that
C^{\mathfrak{k},E}
descends to a parallel section

C^{\mathfrak{k},F}
of
\mathrm{End}\left(F\right)
. Here,
C^{\mathfrak{g},X}
denotes the action of
C^{\mathfrak{g}}
on
C^{\infty}\left(X,S^{TX}\otimes F\right)
.

Here,
D^{X}
denotes
the Dirac operator acting on
C^{\infty}(X,S^{TX}\otimes F)
. If

e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
is an orthonormal basis of
TX
then

(10.3)

D^{X}=\sum_{1}^{n}c\left(e_{i}\right)\nabla^{S^{TX}\otimes F}_{e_{i}}.

Let

\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
be a

\theta
-stable fundamental Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. We will use the notation

(10.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{p}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}=\mathfrak{h}_{\mathfrak{k}}.

Then
\mathfrak{t}\subset\mathfrak{k}
is the Lie algebra of a maximal torus
T\subset K
. Also
\dim\mathfrak{t},\dim\mathfrak{h}
are the complex ranks of

K,G
, and
\dim\mathfrak{b}
is the difference of these complex
ranks. Since
m
is even,
\dim\mathfrak{b}
is also even. Let

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}:Z\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)\simeq I^{\cdot}\left(\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{g}\right)
be the corresponding isomorphism of
Harish-Chandra.

We fix a system of positive roots in
i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
associated
with the pair
\left(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\right)
. In particular

\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
is calculated with respect
to this system.

By
[
B11
, eqs. (7.2.8) and (7.2.9)]
and by
(
2.48
), we get

(10.5)

D^{X,2}=C^{\mathfrak{g},X}-B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{g}},\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}},\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)-C^{\mathfrak{k},F}.

We may and we will assume that
\rho^{E}
is an irreducible representation of
K

with dominant weight
\lambda\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
. Then

(10.6)

C^{\mathfrak{k},E}=-B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda,\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}},\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

By (
10.5
), (
10.6
), we get

(10.7)

D^{X,2}=C^{\mathfrak{g},X}-B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{g}},\rho^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)+B^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda,\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right).

By (
6.29
), we can rewrite (
10.7
) in the form

(10.8)

D^{X,2}=C^{\mathfrak{g},X}-\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}C^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right).

10.2.
The case where
\dim\mathfrak{b}=0

In this Subsection, we assume that
\dim\mathfrak{b}=0
. Then
\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{t}

is a fundamental Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
, and

(10.9)

R=R^{\mathrm{im}}.

Also
R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k}}
is just the root system
associated with the pair
\left(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\right)
. We
fix a positive root system

R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\subset R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k}}
, and
a positive weight system

R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\subset R_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{im}}
which is compatible with the orientation of

\mathfrak{p}
, so that

R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}=R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}
is a positive root system for the
pair
\left(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

The functions

\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}},\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}
on
\mathfrak{t}
are given by

(10.10)

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(h\right)=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}}\left\langle\alpha,h\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(h\right)=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}}\left\langle\alpha,h\right\rangle.

Here,
\rho^{\mathfrak{k}},\lambda\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
are calculated with this
choice of
R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}
. We identify
\mathfrak{t}
and
\mathfrak{t}^{*}
by
the quadratic form
B|_{\mathfrak{t}}
. In particular,

\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}}{2\pi}\right)
and
\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda}{2\pi}\right)
are
well-defined, and
\frac{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda}{2\pi}\right)}{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}}{2\pi}\right)}
only depends on the
orientation of
\mathfrak{p}
. Also

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L
is a polynomial on
\mathfrak{t}^{*}
, and so
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)
is well-defined.

10.3.
Orbital integrals and the index theorem: the case of the
identity

Take
L\in Z(\mathfrak{g})
. For
t>0
,
L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)

acts on
C^{\infty}\left(X,S^{TX}\otimes F\right)
.

In the sequel,

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}
is our notation for the supertrace.
13
13

13

If
V=V_{+}\oplus V_{-}
is a
\mathbf{Z}_{2}
-graded vector space, if
\tau=\pm 1
is
the involution defining the grading, if
A\in\mathrm{End}\left(V\right)
,
the supertrace of
A
is defined to be

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[A\right]=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\tau A\right]
.

We will extend
[
B11
, Theorem 7.4.1]
.
As in
[
B11
, Section 7.1]
,

\widehat{A}\left(TX,\nabla^{TX}\right),\mathrm{ch}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)
denote
the obvious
characteristic forms on
X
. Let
\eta\in\Lambda^{m}\left(T^{*}X\right)
be the canonical volume form on
X

that defines its orientation. If
\alpha\in\Lambda^{\cdot}\left(T^{*}X\right)
, let
\alpha^{(p)}
denote its
component in
\Lambda^{p}\left(T^{*}X\right)
. Let
\alpha^{\max}\in\mathbf{R}
be such that

(10.11)

a^{(m)}=\alpha^{\max}\eta.

Let
\gamma\in G
be
semisimple.

Theorem 10.1
.

If
\gamma
is nonelliptic, for any
t>0
,

(10.12)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=0.

If
\dim\mathfrak{b}>0
, for any
t>0
,

(10.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[1\right]}\left[L\exp(-tD^{X,2})\right]=0,

\displaystyle\left[\widehat{A}\left(TX,\nabla^{TX}\right)\mathrm{ch}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)\right]^{\max}=0.

If
\dim\mathfrak{b}=0
, then

(10.14)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[1\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)\left(-1\right)^{m/2}\frac{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda}{2\pi}\right)}{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}}{2\pi}\right)},

\displaystyle\left[\widehat{A}\left(TX,\nabla^{TX}\right)\mathrm{ch}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)\right]^{\max}=\left(-1\right)^{m/2}\frac{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda}{2\pi}\right)}{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}}{2\pi}\right)}.

Proof.

First we prove (
10.12
). We proceed as in
[
B11
]
. By Proposition
8.5
, by Theorem
9.1
and by
(
10.7
), we get

(10.15)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=\exp\left(-tB^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda,\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)\right)\\
L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(\gamma\right)}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{\gamma}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{a}\right]\left(0\right).

Also

(10.16)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]=\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\\
\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right].

It is well-known that

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]

is a square root of

\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)
.

If
\gamma
is nonelliptic,
a\neq 0
lies in the kernel of

1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and so
(
10.16
) vanishes. By (
10.15
), we get
(
10.12
).

By
[
B11
, Theorem 7.4.1]
, we get

(10.17)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[1\right]}\left[\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=\left[\widehat{A}\left(TX,\nabla^{TX}\right)\mathrm{ch}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)\right]^{\max}.

By (
10.15
), we get

(10.18)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[1\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=\exp\left(-tB^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda,\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)\right)\\
L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{0}\right]\left(0\right).

We will use (
10.16
) with
k=1
.
We have the well-known identity in
[
B11
, eq. (7.5.11)]
,

(10.19)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]=\left(-i\right)^{m/2}\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}}\right]\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}|_{\mathfrak{p}}\right)\right).

In (
10.19
), we may and we will assume that
Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{t}
. Since
\mathfrak{b}\subset\ker\mathrm{ad}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}}
, if
\dim\mathfrak{b}>0
,
then

(10.20)

\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}}\right]=0,

and so (
10.19
) vanishes, which implies the vanishing of
(
10.18
),
i.e., we have established the first identity in (
10.13
).
Combining this equation for
L=1
and (
10.17
), we obtain
the second equation in (
10.13
).

In the sequel, we assume that
\dim\mathfrak{b}=0
.
We will use the notation and results of Subsection
10.2
.

For
\gamma=1
, we use the notation

\mathfrak{g}_{i}=\mathfrak{z}_{i}\left(\gamma\right)
, so that

\mathfrak{g}_{i}=\mathfrak{p}\oplus i\mathfrak{k}
.
Let
L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left(f\right),f\in\mathfrak{g}_{i}
be the
convolution kernel for
L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)
. Then

(10.21)

L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{0}\right]\left(0\right)\\
=\int_{i\mathfrak{k}}L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left(-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]dY_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}.

Let

W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)

14
14

14

As before, we use this
notation instead of
W\left(\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{k}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)

because this Weyl group is real.
denote the Weyl group associated
with the pair
\left(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\right)
. Since the integrated function on
i\mathfrak{k}
is
K
-invariant, by
Weyl’s integration formula, as in (
8.48
), from
(
10.21
), we get

(10.22)

L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}/2\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{0}\right]\left(0\right)\\
=\frac{\mathrm{Vol}\left(K/T\right)}{\left|W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

By (
2.70
), (
10.19
), we get

(10.23)

\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right]=\left(-i\right)^{m/2}\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}}\right]\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

Moreover, given our choice of
R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}
, we have

(10.24)

\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}}\right]=i^{m/2}\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}}\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle.

If
w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)
, let
\epsilon_{w}=\pm 1
be the
determinant of
w
on
\mathfrak{t}
.
Using the Weyl character formula, we have the identity

(10.25)

\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\right|}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}.

By (
10.10
), and (
10.23
)–(
10.25
), we conclude that

(10.26)

\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\right|}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}.

By (
10.26
), we obtain

(10.27)

\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathcal{J}_{1}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\left(\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}\right)dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

As in (
8.40
), we will use Rossmann formula in (
10.27
) with respect
to the par
\left(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{u}\right)
. If
e\in\mathfrak{t}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}
, we get

(10.28)

\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{g}}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{g}}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)e^{-\left\langle e,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\right|}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(\frac{e}{2\pi}\right)L^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(-e\right)\exp\left(tB^{*}\left(e,e\right)\right).

Also
W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)\subset W\left(\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
.
Moreover, if
w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
, if
\epsilon_{w}

still denotes the determinant of
w
on
\mathfrak{t}
, by

[
BrD95
, Corollary V.4.6 and Lemma V.4.10]
,

(10.29)

\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(we\right)=\epsilon_{w}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(e\right).

Finally,
L^{\mathfrak{g}}|_{\mathfrak{t}}
is
W\left(\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbf{C}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}}\right)
-invariant.

By (
10.15
), (
10.22
), (
10.27
), and
(
10.28
), we obtain

(10.30)

\mathrm{Tr}^{\left[1\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]\\
=\mathrm{Vol}\left(K/T\right)\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\right|}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{g}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda}{2\pi}\right)L^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right).

By construction

(10.31)

L^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)=\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right).

By
[
BeGV04
, Corollary 7.27]
, we get

(10.32)

\mathrm{Vol}\left(K/T\right)=\frac{1}{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}}{2\pi}\right)}.

Also

(10.33)

\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\right|=m/2.

By (
10.30
)–(
10.33
), we get the first equation
in (
10.14
).
When
L=1
, we can compare (
10.17
) and
this first equation, and we obtain the second equation in
(
10.14
).
The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 10.2
.

Equation (
10.14
) was obtained by
Atiyah-Schmid
[
ASc77
, eq.
(3.10)]
,
using Hirzebruch proportionality
principle
[
Hi58
]
, together with formulas like
(
10.32
).

10.4.
The case where
\gamma=k^{-1},\dim\mathfrak{b}=0

In this Subsection, we assume that
\gamma
is elliptic, i.e.,

\gamma=k^{-1},k\in K
. Recall that the orientation of
\mathfrak{p}

is fixed.

Let
T\subset K
be a maximal torus, and
let
\mathfrak{t}\subset\mathfrak{k}
be the corresponding Lie
algebra. We may and we will assume that
k\in T
, so that
\mathfrak{t}\subset\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)
. Then
T
is a maximal torus in

K^{0}\left(k\right)
, and
\mathfrak{t}\subset\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)
.
Let
\kappa\in\mathfrak{t}
be such that

(10.34)

k=e^{\kappa}.

Then
\kappa
is well-defined up to the lattice of integral elements
in
\mathfrak{t}
associated with
K
. Since
k
is in the center of

K^{0}\left(k\right)
, if
w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)
,
w\kappa-\kappa
is integral in

\mathfrak{t}
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{b}\oplus\mathfrak{t}
be the
associated fundamental
\theta
-stable Cartan subalgebra of

\mathfrak{g}
. Then
\mathfrak{h}
is a
\theta
-stable fundamental
Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)
.

In this Subsection, we assume that
\dim\mathfrak{b}=0
. Then
\mathfrak{t}
is a

\theta
-stable fundamental Cartan subalgebra of
\mathfrak{g}
and of

\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)
. As in (
10.9
), we have

(10.35)

\displaystyle R=R^{\mathrm{im}},

\displaystyle R\left(k\right)=R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right).

We make the same choice of
R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+},R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}
as in Subsection

10.2
. Set

(10.36)

R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)=R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\cap R^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right).

Then
R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)
is a positive root system associated with
the pair
\left(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)\right)
, and we
still have

(10.37)

R_{+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)=R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)\cup R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right).

The choice of
R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)
defines
an orientation on
\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)
.

The functions
\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)},\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}
on
\mathfrak{t}
are given by

(10.38)

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}\left\langle\alpha,h\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}=\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)}\left\langle\alpha,h\right\rangle.

Again
\rho^{\mathfrak{k}},\lambda\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
are calculated with respect to

R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}
, and

\rho^{\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
is obtained via

R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)
. We identify
\mathfrak{t}
and

\mathfrak{t}^{*}
by the quadratic form
B|_{\mathfrak{t}}
. In
particular
\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}}{2\pi}\right),\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda}{2\pi}\right)

are well-defined as well as

\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)
.

Note that
W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\subset W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)
.
If
w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)
,

e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa\right\rangle}
depends only on the image of

w
in
W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\setminus W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)
.
The same is true for

\epsilon_{w}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)}{2\pi}\right)
.

10.5.
Orbital integrals and index theory: the case of elliptic elements

We use the same notation as in Subsection
10.4
. In
particular,
\gamma=k^{-1},k\in K
.

Let
X\left(\gamma\right)
be the fixed point set of
\gamma
in
X
.
Let

\widehat{A}^{\gamma}\left(TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)},\nabla^{TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)}}\right),\mathrm{ch}^{\gamma}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)

denote the corresponding
Atiyah-Bott characteristic forms on
X\left(\gamma\right)
, that are
defined as in
[
B11
, eqs. (7.7.2) and (7.7.4)]
.

Theorem 10.3
.

If
\dim\mathfrak{b}>0
, then

(10.39)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=0,

\displaystyle\left[\widehat{A}^{\gamma}\left(TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)},\nabla^{TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)}}\right)\mathrm{ch}^{\gamma}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)\right]^{\max}=0.

If
\dim\mathfrak{b}=0
, then

(10.40)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)\\
\left(-1\right)^{\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)/2}\frac{1}{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}}{2\pi}\right)}\frac{1}{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}2\sinh\left(-\left\langle\alpha,\kappa\right\rangle/2\right)}\\
\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\setminus W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)}{2\pi}\right)e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa\right\rangle},

and

(10.41)

\left[\widehat{A}^{\gamma}\left(TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)},\nabla^{TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)}}\right)\mathrm{ch}^{\gamma}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)\right]^{\max}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)/2}\frac{1}{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}}{2\pi}\right)}\frac{1}{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}2\sinh\left(-\left\langle\alpha,\kappa\right\rangle/2\right)}\\
\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\setminus W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)}{2\pi}\right)e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa\right\rangle}.

Proof.

By

[
B11
, Theorem 7.7.1]
, for
t>0
, we get

(10.42)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]\\
=\left[\widehat{A}^{\gamma}\left(TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)},\nabla^{TX|_{X\left(\gamma\right)}}\right),\mathrm{ch}^{\gamma}\left(F,\nabla^{F}\right)\right]^{\max}.

Equation (
10.15
) still
holds. We claim that if
\dim\mathfrak{b}>0
,

(10.43)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]=0.

If
Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)
, after conjugation by an element of

K^{0}\left(k\right)
, we may assume that
Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{t}
. If
\dim\mathfrak{b}>0
, then
1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)
vanishes
on
\mathfrak{b}
. The argument we gave after (
10.16
) shows
that (
10.43
) vanishes. This proves the first equation in
(
10.39
). Combining this equation
for
L=1
and (
10.42
), we get the second equation in (
10.39
).

In the sequel, we assume that
\dim\mathfrak{b}=0
. We will use the notation and results of
Subsection
10.4
, and also equation (
10.15
). As in (
10.21
), and with a similar
notation, we get

(10.44)

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{0}\right]\left(0\right)\\
=\int_{i\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]dY_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}.

Using Weyl integration as in (
10.22
), we deduce from
(
10.44
) that

(10.45)

L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left[\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-Y_{0}^{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\delta_{0}\right]\left(0\right)\\
=\frac{\mathrm{Vol}\left(K^{0}\left(k\right)/T\right)}{\left|W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\right|}\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\\
\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

By
[
B11
, eq. (7.7.7)]
and using the corresponding notation, if
h_{\mathfrak{k}}\in i\mathfrak{t}
, we have the
identity

(10.46)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]=\left(-i\right)^{\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)/2}\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)}\right]\\
\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(\widehat{A}^{ke^{h_{\mathfrak{k}}}|_{\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}\left(0\right)\right)^{-1}.

By (
2.68
), (
10.46
), and by proceeding as in

[
B11
, eq. (7.7.8)]
, we get

(10.47)

\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]=\left(-i\right)^{\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)/2}\\
\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)}\right]\widehat{A}^{-1}\left(\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\right)\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{p}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}\left(0\right)\\
\left[\frac{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}\right]^{1/2}.

Also we have the identity,

(10.48)

\left[\frac{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}{\det\left(1-\mathrm{Ad}\left(k^{-1}\right)\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}\right]^{1/2}=\frac{\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}|_{\mathfrak{k}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}\left(0\right)}{\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}|_{\mathfrak{k}^{\perp}\left(k\right)}}\left(0\right)}.

Using (
10.48
), we can rewrite (
10.47
) in the form,

(10.49)

\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\\
=\left(-i\right)^{\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)/2}\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)}\right]\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}}\left(0\right)\left(\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}}\right)^{-1}\left(-\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}}\right).

As in (
10.24
), we get

(10.50)

\mathrm{Pf}\left[\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)}\right]=i^{\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)/2}\prod_{\alpha\in R_{\mathfrak{p},+}^{\mathrm{im}}\left(k\right)}\left\langle\alpha,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle.

By Weyl’s character formula, we obtain

(10.51)

\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}}\left(-\mathrm{ad}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)|_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr}^{E}\left[\rho^{E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)\right|}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}.

By (
10.49
)–(
10.51
), we find that

(10.52)

\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)\right|}\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}}\left(0\right)\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}.

By (
10.52
), we obtain

(10.53)

\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathcal{J}_{k^{-1}}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left[\rho^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\left(k^{-1}e^{-h_{\mathfrak{k}}}\right)\right]\\
\left[\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\right]^{2}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{k},+}\left(k\right)\right|}\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}}\left(0\right)\\
\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}.

Using Rossmann’s formula as in (
10.28
), if
e\in\mathfrak{t}^{*}_{\mathbf{C}}
, we find that

(10.54)

\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)e^{-\left\langle e,h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)\right|}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{e}{2\pi}\right)L^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(-e\right)\exp\left(tB^{*}\left(e,e\right)\right).

By (
10.54
), we get

(10.55)

\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)\right|}L^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)\exp\left(tB^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda,\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)\right)\\
\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)}{2\pi}\right)e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa\right\rangle}.

By the considerations that follow (
10.34
) and by (
10.55
), we obtain

(10.56)

\int_{i\mathfrak{t}}L^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\exp\left(t\Delta^{\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\right)\left(-h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa+h_{\mathfrak{k}}\right\rangle}dh_{\mathfrak{k}}\\
=\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)\right|}\left|W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\right|L^{\mathfrak{g}}\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)\exp\left(tB^{*}\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda,\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)\right)\\
\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\setminus W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)}{2\pi}\right)e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa\right\rangle}.

By (
10.15
), (
10.45
), (
10.53
), and
(
10.56
), we get

(10.57)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}^{\left[\gamma\right]}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{X,2}\right)\right]=\mathrm{Vol}\left(K^{0}\left(k\right)/T\right)\left(-1\right)^{\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)\right|}\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}}\left(0\right)\\
\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)\sum_{w\in W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)\right)\setminus W\left(\mathfrak{t}:\mathfrak{k}\right)}\epsilon_{w}\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{z}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right)}{2\pi}\right)e^{-\left\langle w\left(\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda\right),\kappa\right\rangle}.

As in (
10.32
), we obtain

(10.58)

\mathrm{Vol}\left(K^{0}\left(k\right)/T\right)=\frac{1}{\pi^{\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}\left(\frac{\rho^{\mathfrak{k}\left(k\right)}}{2\pi}\right)}.

As in (
10.33
), we get

(10.59)

\left|R^{\mathrm{im}}_{\mathfrak{p},+}\left(k\right)\right|=\dim\mathfrak{p}\left(k\right)/2.

Moreover,

(10.60)

\widehat{A}^{k^{-1}}\left(0\right)=\frac{1}{\prod_{\alpha\in R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\setminus R^{\mathrm{im}}_{+}\left(k\right)}2\sinh\left(-\left\langle\alpha,\kappa\right\rangle/2\right)}.

By (
10.57
)–(
10.60
), we
get (
10.40
), which combined with (
10.42
) gives (
10.41
). The proof of our theorem is completed.
∎

Remark 10.4
.

Equation (
10.41
) can also be obtained by a suitable
application of Hirzebruch proportionality principle

[
Hi58
]
similar to what was done by Atiyah-Schmid

[
ASc77
]
.

10.6.
Orbital integrals and a conjecture by Vogan

Let
\pi
be an irreducible unitary representation of
G
acting on a
Hilbert space
V_{\pi}
. By
[
Kn86
, p. 205]
, a vector

v\in V_{\pi}
is called
K
-finite if the vector subspace
generated by the vectors
kv|_{k\in K}
has finite
dimension. Let
V_{\pi,K}\subset V_{\pi}
be the vector subspace
of the
K
-finite
vectors in
V_{\pi}
. By
[
Kn86
, Proposition 8.5]
,
U(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}})
acts on

V_{\pi,K}
, so that
V_{\pi,K}
is a
(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathbf{C}},K)
-module.

Let
e_{1},\ldots,e_{m}
be
an orthonormal basis of
\mathfrak{p}
. Let
D\in U(\mathfrak{g})\otimes c(\mathfrak{p})
be the Dirac operator,

(10.61)

\displaystyle D=\sum_{i=1}^{m}c(e_{i})e_{i}.

We denote by
D|_{V_{\pi,K}\otimes S^{\mathfrak{p}}}
the restriction
of
D
to

V_{\pi,K}\otimes S^{\mathfrak{p}}
. By
[
HuP02
, p. 189]
, the Dirac
cohomology of
V_{\pi,K}
is the
K
-module defined by

(10.62)

\displaystyle H_{D}(V_{\pi,K})=\ker D|_{V_{\pi,K}\otimes S^{\mathfrak{p}}}.

By
[
Kn86
, Theorem 8.1]
, each
K
-type in
H_{D}(V_{\pi,K})
has finite multiplicity.

The Vogan conjecture, solved by Huang-Pandžić

[
HuP02
, Corollary 2.4]
states the following.

Theorem 10.5
.

If the Dirac cohomology
H_{D}(V_{\pi,K})
contains a
K
-type of
highest weight
\lambda\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
, the infinitesimal
character of
V_{\pi,K}
is
\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}+\lambda
.

An equivalent formulation of Theorem
10.5
says that if
E
is an irreducible
K
representation of highest
weight
\lambda\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
, if
D^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}
denotes the restriction of
D
to
(V_{\pi,K}\otimes S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E)^{K}
, if
\ker D^{S^{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes E}\neq 0
, then
L\in Z(\mathfrak{g})
acts on
V_{\pi,K}
as the
scalar
\phi_{\rm HC}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)
.

We will show that (
10.14
) and
(
10.40
) are compatible with Theorem

10.5
. Let
\Gamma
be a discrete cocompact subgroup
of
G
. By
[
GGP90
, Theorem, p.23]
, we have

(10.63)

\displaystyle L^{2}(\Gamma\backslash G)=\bigoplus_{\pi\in\widehat{G}_{u}}^{\rm Hil}n_{\Gamma}(\pi)V_{\pi},

with
n_{\Gamma}(\pi)\in\mathbf{N}
.

We use the notation of Subsection
10.1
. In particular, we assume that

\rho^{E}
is an irreducible representation of
K
with highest weight

\lambda\in i\mathfrak{t}^{*}
. Let
Z
be the compact orbifold

Z=\Gamma\setminus X
. The vector bundle
F
on
X
descends to an
orbifold
vector bundle on
Z
, which we still denote
F
. Also
D^{X}

descends to the orbifold Dirac operator
D^{Z}
. By (
10.63
), we have

(10.64)

\ker D^{Z}=\bigoplus_{\pi\in\widehat{G}_{u}}n_{\Gamma}(\pi)(H_{D}(V_{\pi,K})\otimes E)^{K}.

Since
\ker D^{Z}
is finite-dimensional, the sum in the right-hand
side only contains finitely many nonzero terms. By Theorem
10.5
,
L\in Z(\mathfrak{g})
acts on
\ker D^{Z}
as
\phi_{\rm HC}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)
.

Using the McKean-Singer formula
[
MS67
]
and
the above, for
t>0
, we get

(10.65)

\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{Z,2}\right)\right]=\phi_{\mathrm{HC}}L\left(-\rho^{\mathfrak{k}}-\lambda\right)\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[\exp\left(-tD^{Z,2}\right)\right].

Also
\mathrm{Tr_{s}}\left[L\exp\left(-tD^{Z,2}\right)\right]
can be evaluated
in terms of corresponding orbital integrals using Selberg’s trace
formula.

Assume first that
\Gamma
is torsion free.
By equation (
10.12
) in
Theorem
10.1
, only the identity element contributes to the
above supertrace. Then equation (
10.14
) can be viewed as a
consequence of (
10.17
), (
10.65
).

When
\Gamma
is not torsion free, only the finite number of conjugacy
classes of elliptic elements in
\Gamma
contribute to
(
10.65
). Then equations (
10.40
), (
10.42
)
are compatible to (
10.65
).
